for all
n
, CENTREx (
\widehat{\bm{\Delta}}
) is CENTREx with the
{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}
equal to estimates
\widehat{\bm{\Delta}}_{n}
of the
\bm{\Delta}_{n}
and, for different
\widehat{\sigma}
, CENTREx (
\widehat{\sigma}^{2}\bm{I}_{{\mathrm{d}}}
) is CENTREx with all the
{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}
equal to
\widehat{\sigma}^{2}\bm{I}_{{\mathrm{d}}}
: (a) Percentage of correctly retrieved number of clusters; (b) Silhouette; (c) Error rate; (d) Average estimated value of
K
; (e) zoom on error rate.

8.3
Clustering performance on standard data sets

We also evaluate the performance of our algorithm on the two standard Machine Learning data sets Ruspini and IRIS. On both data sets, CENTREx is used under the assumption that the clusters have same covariance matrix equal to
\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{{\mathrm{d}}}
. It employs
{\widehat{\sigma}_{\text{MLE}}}
according to Subsection
7.2
to estimate
\sigma
, with parameter values
M\!=\!P=\!75
(resp.
M\!=\!P\!=\!10
) in
Equation 42
for the Ruspini data set (resp. the Iris data set) and
\bm{C}\!=\!\bm{I}_{{\mathrm{d}}}
again in
Equation 44
for the two data sets. We set
L=100
maximum iterations for centroid estimation, and set
\epsilon_{e}=10^{-3}
,
\epsilon_{f}=1
,
\alpha=10^{-3}
.

On the Ruspini data set, our algorithm correctly retrieves
K=4
clusters, like most clustering algorithms. On the IRIS data set, it finds
K=2
clusters while, in true, IRIS contains three classes. This is probably due to the IRIS data set geometry, since other clustering algorithms such as K-means with unknown
K
also retrieve
K=2
clusters on IRIS.

(a)

(b)

Figure 6:
Clustering with CENTREx on (a) Ruspini, (b) IRIS (
2
nd and
4
th dimensions). The red dots are the estimated centroids.

8.4
Brief summary and discussion

Under our experimental set-up, the clustering algorithms with no prior knowledge of the number of clusters encounter difficulties to properly cluster data for
\sigma_{\text{min}}\geqslant 30
. Indeed, in our simulations, we set a minimum distance
\mu_{\min}=200
between any two centroids. With the Gaussian model,
99\%
of the values taken by measurements of a specified cluster in a given direction are thus within
3\sigma
of the cluster centroid. So, when
\sigma
draws near
30
,
3\sigma\approx 90
approaches
\mu_{\min}/2=100
, inducing that measurements from two clusters at a distance equal to
\mu_{\text{min}}
can mingle enough in certain directions to mislead clustering algorithms that have no prior knowledge of
K
.

The simulation results presented above show that CENTREx, even when used with estimates of the measurement covariance matrices, is a relevant alternative to K-means++, Mean-Shift and X-means, when the number of clusters is unknown. These results suggest that a large difference between the numbers of centroids estimated by CENTREx and X-means respectively, means that the clusters are likely mixing too much to be separated without too many errors.

In addition, considering the Wald kernel instead of the Gaussian kernel leads to significant gains in terms of clustering performance for both CENTREx and Mean-Shift. Finally, with a reduced complexity for CENTREx compared to Mean-Shift, when the covariance matrices of the measurements are all estimated by the same scaled-identity matrix, CENTREx and Mean-Shift achieve similar performance. All these experimental results emphasize the relevance of Theorem
1
to estimate centroids by seeking the fixed-points of
\bm{G}_{N}
, even in non-asymptotic cases.

Like Mean-Shift, CENTREx and the underlying theory introduced in this paper are devised to handle Gaussian and sufficiently well-separated clusters. Although the experimental results above are promising regarding the ability of CENTREx to deal with estimates for the covariance matrices of the measurement vectors, the robustness of this algorithm should be further investigated to identify more precisely the relevant classes of estimators that could be used with it. The performance of CENTREx is also sensitive to the parameter
\epsilon_{f}
used for the final fusion step required by Mean-Shift as well. Further studies on means to better control and mitigate the influence of this parameter are also required.

9
Conclusion and perspectives

In this paper, we have addressed the problem of clustering heteroscedastic Gaussian measurement vectors when the number
K
of clusters is unknown. First, we have proposed a theoretical framework from which we have derived Theorem
1
showing that the fixed-points of an extended and randomized version of the mean-shift function
(
9
)
converge, in a probabilistic sense introduced in this paper, to the cluster centroids.

From these theoretical results, we have derived a new clustering algorithm called CENTREx, which does not require any prior knowledge of
K
. CENTREx can be regarded as an extension of Mean-Shift with reduced complexity. We also introduced the Wald kernel, defined as the p-value of a Wald hypothesis test, in order to replace the Gaussian kernel usually considered in the literature. Extensive simulation results on synthetic data have been presented to get better insight into the behaviour and robustness of CENTREx in non-asymptotic situations.

Our approach opens several prospects to be addressed in future works. First, the properties of our extended and randomized mean-shift function
\bm{G}_{N}
could be further investigated to prove that the recursive routine used in our algorithm converges to its fixed-points. In this respect, it can be asked whether the theoretical frameworks established in
16
and
56
can be combined with ours to yield a modified version of CENTREx, based on the Wald kernel and involving a convergence-guaranteed fixed-point search. On the other hand, because our theory and CENTREx algorithm extend the Mean-Shift theory and algorithm, the Mean-Shift variants in
16
and
56
might in turn benefit from our theoretical results and the use of the Wald kernel. Finally, since our theoretical framework addresses the case of measurement vectors with possibly different covariance matrices, the theory and the resulting algorithm CENTREx provide us with a potential framework for distributed clustering over sensor networks, either in the centralized or the decentralized case.

Appendix A
Proof of Theorem
1

This proof is based on many intermediate results, whose proofs are detailed as much as possible for the reader to follow the computations and arguments without undue effort. To alleviate its reading, we split the proof in four parts. In Section
A.1
, we first state preliminary properties of the
ess.lim.sup
defined in Definition
7
. In Section
A.2
, we establish
(\textbf{A})
in the particular case of any sequences
\bm{C}\!=\!(\bm{C})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and
{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}\!=\!({{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
of diagonal covariance matrices, that is, when
\bm{R}=\bm{I}_{{\mathrm{d}}}
in
Equation 27
. From this particular case, we derive
(\textbf{A})
for non-diagonal covariance matrices in Section
A.3
and
(\textbf{B})
in Section
A.4
.

A.1
Preliminary properties of
ess.lim.sup

We begin with Proposition
2
to express the
ess.lim.sup
in the form of a predicate. From this predicate, we derive useful properties that will be helpful to prove Theorem
1
. To establish Proposition
2
, we recall basic properties of a limsup in Lemma
2
.

Lemma 2
.

Given any sequence
(x_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
of real numbers and any
\eta\in{]0,\infty[}
,

\displaystyle\underset{N\to\infty}{\overline{\lim}}x_{N}<\eta\Rightarrow\big(\exists N_{0}\in\mathbb{N},\forall N\in\mathbb{N},N>N_{0}\Rightarrow x_{N}<\eta\big)\,,

(46a)

\displaystyle\big(\exists N_{0}\in\mathbb{N},\forall N\in\mathbb{N},N>N_{0}\Rightarrow x_{N}<\eta\big)\Rightarrow\underset{N\to\infty}{\overline{\lim}}x_{N}\leqslant\eta\,.

(46b)

We also remind the reader that, for any random variable
X\in\mathcal{M}(\Omega,\mathbb{R})
and any
\eta\in{[0,\infty[}
(see
40
):

\begin{array}[]{lll}|X|_{\infty}\leqslant\eta&\Leftrightarrow&|X|\leqslant\eta\,\,\text{\big($\mathbb{P}$-a.s\big)}\\
&\Leftrightarrow&\exists\,\Omega_{0}\in\mathcal{A},\big(\,\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{0})=1\wedge(\forall\omega\in\Omega_{0},|X(\omega)|\leqslant\eta)\,\big)\,.\end{array}

(47)

Proposition 2
.

Given any parametric discrete random process
\bm{F}:\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{M}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}})
, any base
\mathscr{B}
in
\mathbb{X}
, any
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
and any norm
\nu
on
\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathscr{B}}\big|\underset{\,N\to\infty}{\overline{\lim}}\nu(\bm{F}(x,N)-\zeta\,)\,\big|_{\infty}=0

(48)

if and only if,

\begin{array}[]{lll}\forall\eta\in{]0,\infty[},\exists B\in\mathscr{B},\\
\hskip 42.67912pt\forall x\in\mathbb{X},x\in B\Rightarrow\exists\,\Omega_{0}\in\mathcal{A},\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\!\!\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{0})=1\\
\!\!\text{\large$\bm{\wedge}$}\\
\!\!\forall\omega\in\Omega_{0},\exists N_{0}\in\mathbb{N},\forall N\in\mathbb{N},\\
\hskip 4.26773ptN>N_{0}\Rightarrow\|\bm{F}(x,N)(\omega)-\zeta\|<\eta\,.\end{array}\right.\end{array}\hskip-17.07182pt

(49)

Proof.

Consider a base
\mathscr{B}
in
\mathbb{X}
,
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
and a norm
\nu
on
\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
. Suppose first that

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathscr{B}}\big|\underset{\,N\to\infty}{\overline{\lim}}\nu(\bm{F}(x,N)-\zeta\,)\,\big|_{\infty}=0\,.

In
Equation 17
, by equivalently replacing the strict inequality by a slack one and considering
f(x)=\big|\underset{N\to\infty}{\overline{\lim}}\nu\!\left(\bm{F}(x,N)-\zeta\right)\,\big|_{\infty}
and the absolute value as norm on
\mathbb{R}
, we obtain:

\forall\eta\in{]0,\infty[},\exists B\in\mathscr{B},\forall x\in\mathbb{X},x\in B\Rightarrow\big|\,\underset{N\to\infty}{\overline{\lim}}\nu\!\left(\bm{F}(x,N)-\zeta\right)\,\big|_{\infty}\leqslant\eta/2\,.

According to
Equation 47
, we can rewrite the predicate above as

\forall\eta\in{]0,\infty[},\exists B\in\mathscr{B},\forall x\in\mathbb{X},x\in B\Rightarrow\underset{N\to\infty}{\overline{\lim}}\nu\!\left(\bm{F}(x,N)-\zeta\right)\leqslant\eta/2\,\,\text{\big($\mathbb{P}$-a.s\big)}\,,

and thus, via
Equation 47
again, it follows that

\begin{array}[]{lll}\forall\eta\in{]0,\infty[},\exists B\in\mathscr{B},\\
\hskip 34.14322pt\forall x\in\mathbb{X},x\in B\Rightarrow\exists\,\Omega_{0}\in\mathcal{A},\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\!\!\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{0})=1\\
\!\!\text{\large$\bm{\wedge}$}\\
\!\!\forall\omega\in\Omega_{0},\displaystyle\underset{\,N\to\infty}{\overline{\lim}}\nu\!\left(\bm{F}(x,N)(\omega)-\zeta\right)\leqslant\eta/2<\eta\end{array}\right.\,.\end{array}

We obtain
Equation 49
by injecting
Equation 46a
into the predicate above and taking into account that all norms on
\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
are equivalent.

Conversely, if
Equation 49
holds true, we derive from
Equation 46b
and the equivalence of norms on
\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
that

\forall\eta^{\prime}\in{]0,\infty[},\exists B\in\mathscr{B},\forall x\in\mathbb{X},x\in B\,\Rightarrow\exists\,\Omega_{0}\in\mathcal{A},\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\!\!\!\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{0})=1\\
\!\!\!\text{\large$\bm{\wedge}$}\\
\!\!\!\forall\omega\!\in\!\Omega_{0},\displaystyle\underset{N\to\infty}{\overline{\lim}}\nu\!\left(\bm{F}(x,N)\!-\!\zeta\right)\!\leqslant\!\eta^{\prime}\end{array}\right.\,.

This predicate is equivalent to

\forall\eta^{\prime}\in{]0,\infty[},\exists B\in\mathscr{B},\forall x\in\mathbb{X},x\in B\Rightarrow\displaystyle\underset{N\to\infty}{\overline{\lim}}\nu\!\left(\bm{F}(x,N)-\zeta\right)\leqslant\eta^{\prime}\,\,\text{\big($\mathbb{P}$-a.s\big)}\,,

(50)

and hence, according to
Equation 47
, to:

\forall\eta^{\prime}\in{]0,\infty[},\exists B\in\mathscr{B},\forall x\in\mathbb{X},x\in B\Rightarrow\Big|\displaystyle\underset{N\to\infty}{\overline{\lim}}\nu\!\left(\bm{F}(x,N)-\zeta\right)\Big|_{\infty}\leqslant\eta^{\prime}\,.

(51)

Consider any
\eta\in{]0,\infty[}
and any
\eta^{\prime}\in{]0,\eta[}
. It follows from
Equation 51
that

\exists B\!\in\!\mathscr{B},\forall x\!\in\!\mathbb{X},x\!\in\!B\Rightarrow\Big|\underset{N\to\infty}{\overline{\lim}}\nu\!\left(\bm{F}(x,N)-\zeta\right)\Big|_{\infty}\leqslant\eta^{\prime}<\eta\,,

which concludes the proof.
∎

Corollary 1
.

If
\bm{F}:\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{M}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}})
is a parametric discrete random process then, for any base
\mathscr{B}
in
\mathbb{X}
, any
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
and any norm
\nu
on
\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
,

\displaystyle\mathop{\text{ess. lim. sup}}_{\mathscr{B}\,,\,N\to\infty}\bm{F}(x,N)=\zeta\,\,\,\Leftrightarrow\,\,\displaystyle\lim_{\mathscr{B}}\big|\underset{\,N\to\infty}{\overline{\lim}}\nu(\bm{F}(x,N)-\zeta\,)\,\big|_{\infty}=0\,.

Proof.

By Definition
7
,
\displaystyle\mathop{\text{ess. lim. sup}}_{\mathscr{B}\,,\,N\to\infty}\bm{F}(x,N)=\zeta
is equivalent to

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathscr{B}}\left|\,\underset{\,N\to\infty}{\overline{\lim}}\|\,\bm{F}(x,N)-\zeta\,\|_{\,}\right|_{\infty}=0\,.

(52)

In the particular case of the Euclidean norm
\|\cdot\|
, Proposition
2
implies that
Equation 52
is equivalent to
Equation 49
. Thence the result by Proposition
2
.
∎

Consider any
\bm{A}\in\operatorname{GL}_{\mathrm{d}}(\mathbb{R})
. We define a norm on
\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
by setting
\nu(\bm{x})=\|\bm{A}\bm{x}\,\|
for each
\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
. Therefore, we obtain the following statement from Corollary
1
.

Proposition 3
.

If
\bm{F}:\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{M}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}})
is a parametric discrete random process and
\bm{A}\in\operatorname{GL}_{\mathrm{d}}(\mathbb{R})
then, for any base
\mathscr{B}
in
\mathbb{X}
and any
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
,

\displaystyle\mathop{\text{ess. lim. sup}}_{\mathscr{B}\,,\,N\to\infty}\bm{F}(x,N)=\zeta\,\,\Leftrightarrow\,\,\displaystyle\mathop{\text{ess. lim. sup}}_{\mathscr{B}\,,\,N\to\infty}\bm{A}\bm{F}(x,N)=\bm{A}\zeta\,.

The next two results are also direct consequences of Proposition
2
with
\nu=\|\cdot\|
.

Proposition 4
.

If
\bm{F}:\!\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{M}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}})
is a parametric discrete random process and if, for any
i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket
,
\bm{F}_{i}:\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{M}(\Omega,\mathbb{R})
is defined by:

\begin{array}[]{llll}\bm{F}_{i}:&\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{N}&\to&\mathcal{M}(\Omega,\mathbb{R})\\
&(x,N)&\mapsto&(\bm{F}(x,N))_{i}\end{array}

then, for any base
\mathscr{B}
in
\mathbb{X}
and any
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
,

\displaystyle\mathop{\text{ess. lim. sup}}_{\mathscr{B}\,,\,N\to\infty}\bm{F}(x,N)=\zeta\,\,\Leftrightarrow\,\,\forall i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket,\displaystyle\mathop{\text{ess. lim. sup}}_{\mathscr{B}\,,\,N\to\infty}\bm{F}_{i}(x,N)=\zeta_{i}\,.

Proposition 5
.

Consider any two parametric discrete random processes
\bm{F},\bm{F}^{\prime}:\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{M}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}})
.
Given a base
\mathscr{B}
in
\mathbb{X}
, a pair
(\zeta,\zeta^{\prime})\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}\times\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
and any
{(\alpha,\alpha^{\prime})}\in\mathbb{R}
,

\begin{array}[]{lll}\hskip-28.45274pt\Big(\,\big(\,\displaystyle\mathop{\text{ess. lim. sup}}_{\mathscr{B}\,,\,N\to\infty}\bm{F}(x,N)=\zeta\,\big)\hskip 2.84544pt\text{\large$\bm{\wedge}$}\hskip 2.84544pt\big(\,\displaystyle\mathop{\text{ess. lim. sup}}_{\mathscr{B}\,,\,N\to\infty}\bm{F}^{\prime}(x,N)=\zeta^{\prime}\,\big)\,\Big)\\
\hskip 56.9055pt\Rightarrow\hskip 5.69046pt\displaystyle\mathop{\text{ess. lim. sup}}_{\mathscr{B}\,,\,N\to\infty}\left(\alpha\bm{F}(x,N)+{\alpha^{\prime}}\bm{F}^{\prime}(x,N)\right)=\alpha\zeta+{\alpha^{\prime}}{\zeta^{\prime}}\,,\end{array}

with

\begin{array}[]{llll}\forall{\lambda}\in\mathbb{R},{\lambda}\bm{F}(x,N):&\Omega&\to&\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}\\
&\omega&\mapsto&{\lambda}\bm{F}(x,N)(\omega)\,.\end{array}

The following corollary of Proposition
2
and
Equation 24
is direct.

Corollary 2

(of Proposition
2
)
.

Given any
(\bm{\varphi},\bm{\varphi}_{0})\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}\times\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
, any
k_{0}\in\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket
, any parametric discrete random process
\bm{F}:{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}\times\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{M}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}})
,

\displaystyle\mathop{\text{ess. lim. sup}}_{D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})\to\infty\,,\,N\to\infty}\bm{F}(\bm{\Phi},N)=\bm{\varphi}

if and only if,

\begin{array}[]{lll}\hskip-19.91684pt\forall\eta\in{]0,\infty[},\exists\,r\in{]0,\infty[},\forall\bm{\Phi}\in{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},\\
\hskip 21.33955ptD^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})>r\Rightarrow\exists\,\Omega_{0}\in\mathcal{A},\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\!\!\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{0})=1\\
\!\!\text{\large$\bm{\wedge}$}\\
\!\!\forall\omega\in\Omega_{0},\exists N_{0}\in\mathbb{N},\\
\hskip 5.69046pt\forall N\in\mathbb{N},N>N_{0}\Rightarrow\|\bm{F}(\bm{\Phi},N)(\omega)-\bm{\varphi}\,\|<\eta\,.\end{array}\right.\end{array}\hskip-28.45274pt

(53)

A.2
Proof of equivalence
(\textbf{A})
for diagonal positive-definite matrices

From now on, consider a surjective sequence
\kappa\!=\!(\kappa_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
of integers valued in
\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket
, a function
w:\!{[0,\infty[}\!\to\!{]0,\infty[}
and two sequences
\bm{C}=(\bm{C}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and
{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}=({{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
of positive definite diagonal matrices satisfying
1
,
2
,
3
and
4
. We set:

\forall n\in\mathbb{N},\bm{C}_{n}=\operatorname{diag}(\,{\sigma^{\,2}_{\!n,1},\ldots,\sigma^{\,2}_{\!n,\mathrm{d}}}\,)\,.

(54)

We thus have:

\forall n\in\mathbb{N},\forall i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket,\sigma_{n,i}\in[\sigma_{\text{min}},\sigma_{\text{max}}]\,.

(55)

Similarly, we put

\forall n\in\mathbb{N},{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}=\operatorname{diag}(\,{\sigma^{\prime}}^{\,2}_{\!\!\!n,1},\ldots,{\sigma^{\prime}}^{\,2}_{\!\!\!n,\mathrm{d}}\,)\,.

(56)

and we have:

\forall n\in\mathbb{N},\forall i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket,{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!n,i}\in[{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!\text{min}},{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!\text{max}}]\,.

(57)

For any sequence
\bm{W}=(\bm{W}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
of elements of
\mathcal{M}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}})
, any
N\in\mathbb{N}
, any
\bm{\varphi}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
and any
i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket
, we set:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lll}\bm{U}_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{W})=\dfrac{1}{N}\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{N}w\big(\nu_{{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}(\bm{W}_{n}-\bm{\varphi})\big){\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!n,i}^{\,-2}(\bm{W}_{n}-\bm{\varphi})_{i}\,,\end{array}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lll}V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{W})=\dfrac{1}{N}\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{N}w\big(\nu_{{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}(\bm{W}_{n}-\bm{\varphi})\big){\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!n,i}^{\,-2}\,.\end{array}

It follows from
Equation 13
&
Equation 58
that:

\bm{G}_{N}^{{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{W})-\bm{\varphi}=\left(\dfrac{\bm{U}_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{W})}{V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{W})}\right)_{\!1\leqslant i\leqslant\mathrm{d}}\,.

(59)

According to
Equation 25
, when
N
and
D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})
tend to
\infty
, the convergence in
ess.lim.sup
of
\bm{G}_{N}^{{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))
to
\bm{\varphi}
is equivalent to that of
\bm{G}_{N}^{{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))-\bm{\varphi}
to
0
. Hence,
Equation 59
and Proposition
4
show that, when
N
and
D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})
tend to
\infty
, the convergence in
ess.lim.sup
of
\bm{G}_{N}^{{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))
to
\bm{\varphi}
boils down to that of each

\frac{\bm{U}_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))}{V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))}
to
0
for
1\leqslant i\leqslant\mathrm{d}
.
The proof thus articulates around the analyzis, given
i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket
, of
\bm{U}_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))
and
V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))
, when
N
and
D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})
tend to
\infty
. This analyzis is achieved through Lemmas
3
to
5
. These results are then combined to state Lemma
6
. Corollary
4
of Lemma
6
is then used to conclude the proof with the help of Lemma
7
whose crucial role to achieve the proof can hardly be construed at this stage.

To proceed, we define

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\hskip-2.84544pt\forall k\in\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket,\mathcal{C}_{k}=\left\{n\in\mathbb{N}:\kappa_{n}=k)\right\}\,,\\
\hskip-2.84544pt\forall(k,N)\in\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket\times{\llbracket K,\infty\llbracket},\mathcal{C}_{k,N}=\mathcal{C}_{k}\cap\llbracket 1,N\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket\,.\end{array}\right.

(60)

and set also

\hskip-5.69046pt\forall(k,N)\!\in\!\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket\!\times\!{\llbracket K,\infty\llbracket},a_{k,N}=\operatorname{card}\!\left(\mathcal{C}_{k,N}\right)/N\geqslant a_{0}\,,

(61)

where the inequality in
Equation 61
directly follows from
1
.

Given any
\bm{\varphi}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
and any sequence
\bm{W}=(\bm{W}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
of
\mathrm{d}
-dimensional real random vectors, we also define, for any
k\in\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket
, any
N\in{\llbracket K,\infty\llbracket}
and any
i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket
,

\displaystyle S_{k,N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{W})=\dfrac{1}{\operatorname{card}\!\left(\mathcal{C}_{k,N}\right)}\displaystyle\sum_{n\in\mathcal{C}_{k,N}}\hskip-2.84544ptw\big(\nu_{{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}(\bm{W}_{n}-\bm{\varphi})\big)\,{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!n,i}^{\,-2}\left(\bm{W}_{n}-\bm{\varphi}\right)_{i}\,,

(62a)

\displaystyle T_{k,N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{W})=\dfrac{1}{\operatorname{card}\!\left(\mathcal{C}_{k,N}\right)}\hskip-7.11317pt\displaystyle\sum_{\hskip 8.19447ptn\in\mathcal{C}_{k,N}}\hskip-8.5359ptw\big(\nu_{{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}(\bm{W}_{n}-\bm{\varphi})\big)\,{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!n,i}^{\,-2}\,.

(62b)

With this notation, we have:

\displaystyle\hskip 2.84544pt\bm{U}_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{W})=\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}a_{k,N}S_{k,N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{W})\,,

(63a)

\displaystyle\hskip 2.84544ptV_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{W})=\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}a_{k,N}T_{k,N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{W})\,.

(63b)

To ease the reading, we remind the reader that
{\mathbb{S}}
,
{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}}
, and
{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}})}
, are defined by Definitions
1
,
2
and
3
, respectively.

Lemma 3
.

Given any
\bm{Y}\in{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}})}
, any
\bm{\Phi}\in{\mathbb{S}}
, any
\bm{\varphi}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
and any
i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket
, we have
(
\mathbb{P}
-a.s
)
:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\left(\bm{U}_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))-\mathbb{E}\big[\bm{U}_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\right)=0\,,\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0pt

\displaystyle\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\left(V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))-\mathbb{E}\big[V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\right)=0\,.

Proof.

For any
\bm{Y}\in{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}})}
, any
\bm{\Phi}\in{\mathbb{S}}
, any
\bm{\varphi}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
, any
i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket
, any
\omega\in\Omega
and any
N\in\mathbb{N}
, it follows from
Equation 63
that:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lll}\big|\,\bm{U}_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))(\omega)-\mathbb{E}\big[\,\bm{U}_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\,\big]\big|\\
\hskip 28.45274pt\leqslant\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}a_{k,N}\big|\,S_{k,N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))(\omega)-\mathbb{E}\big[\,S_{k,N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\,\big]\big|\,,\end{array}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lll}\hskip-2.84544pt\big|\,V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))(\omega)-\mathbb{E}\big[\,V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\,\big]\big|\\
\hskip 28.45274pt\leqslant\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}a_{k,N}\big|\,T_{k,N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))(\omega)-\mathbb{E}\big[\,T_{k,N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\,\big]\big|\,,\end{array}

According to
44
or
44
, and since
Equation 61
implies that
\operatorname{card}\!\left(\mathcal{C}_{k,N}\right)
grows to
\infty
when
N
grows itself to
\infty
, we can write that, for any
k\in\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket
,

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\left(S_{k,N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))-\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k,N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\right)=0\,\,\text{\big($\mathbb{P}$-a.s\big)}\,.

Thus, for any
k\in\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket
, there exists
\Omega^{\prime}_{k}\in\mathcal{A}
such that
\mathbb{P}(\Omega^{\prime}_{k})=1
and, for any
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime}_{k}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\left(S_{k,N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))(\omega)-\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k,N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\right)=0\,.

(66)

Since
\mathbb{P}(\Omega^{\prime})=1
with
\Omega^{\prime}=\bigcap_{k=1}^{K}\Omega^{\prime}_{k}
, the foregoing establishes the existence of
\Omega^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}
such that
\mathbb{P}(\Omega^{\prime})=1
and such that
Equation 66
holds true for any
k\in\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket
and any
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime}
.
Hence, for any
k\in\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket
, any
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime}
and any
\eta\in{]0,\infty[}
, there exists
N^{\prime}_{k,\omega,\eta}\in\mathbb{N}
such that:

\forall N\in\mathbb{N},N>N^{\prime}_{k,\omega,\eta}\Rightarrow\left|S_{k,N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))(\omega)-\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k,N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\right|<\eta\,.

(67)

From
Equation 65
,
Equation 67
and the definition of
a_{k,N}
given by
Equation 61
, we conclude to the existence of
\Omega^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}
with
\mathbb{P}(\Omega^{\prime})=1
such that, for any
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime}
and any
\eta\in{]0,\infty[}
, there exists
N^{\prime}_{\omega,\eta}=\max\big\{N^{\prime}_{k,\omega,\eta}:k\in\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket\big\}\in\mathbb{N}
for which we have

\forall N\in\mathbb{N},N>N^{\prime}_{\omega,\eta}\Rightarrow\left|\bm{U}_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))(\omega)-\mathbb{E}\big[\bm{U}_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\right|<\eta\,.

(68)

By a similar reasoning left to the reader, we establish the existence of
\Omega^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{A}
such that
\mathbb{P}(\Omega^{\prime\prime})=1
and such that, for any
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime\prime}
and any
\eta\in{]0,\infty[}
, there exists
N^{\prime\prime}_{\omega,\eta}\in\mathbb{N}
for which we have

\forall N\in\mathbb{N},N>N^{\prime\prime}_{\omega,\eta}\Rightarrow\left|V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))(\omega)-\mathbb{E}\big[V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\right|<\eta\,.

(69)

Equation 68
and
Equation 69
establish the existence of
\Omega_{0}=\Omega^{\prime}\cap\Omega^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{A}
such that
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{0})=1
and such that, for any
\omega\in\Omega_{0}
and any
\eta\in{]0,\infty[}
, there exists
N_{0}=\max\{N^{\prime}_{\omega,\eta},N^{\prime\prime}_{\omega,\eta}\}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\forall N\in\mathbb{N},N>N_{0}\Rightarrow\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\hskip-2.84544pt\left|\,\bm{U}_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))(\omega)-\mathbb{E}\big[\bm{U}_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\,\right|<\eta\,,\\
\hskip-2.84544pt\left|\,V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))(\omega)-\mathbb{E}\big[V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\,\right|<\eta\,.\end{array}\right.

Thence the result.
∎

For all
\bm{Y}\in{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}})}
,
\bm{\Phi}\in{\mathbb{S}}
,
\bm{\varphi}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
and
i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket
, the result above provides us with the behavior of
\bm{U}_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))-\mathbb{E}\big[\bm{U}_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]
and
V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))-\mathbb{E}\big[V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]
when
N
tends to
\infty
. In what follows, we address the behaviour of
\mathbb{E}\big[\bm{U}_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]
and
\mathbb{E}\big[V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]
when
D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})\to\infty
.

As of now, given
\bm{\varphi}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
and
r\in{[0,\infty[}
,
\mathcal{B}(\bm{\varphi},r)=\{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}:\|\bm{x}-\bm{\varphi}\|\leqslant r\}
denotes the (closed) ball centred at
\bm{\varphi}
with radius
r
. In addition,
\mathcal{B}(\bm{\varphi},r)^{c}
designates the complementary of
\mathcal{B}(\bm{\varphi},r)
in
\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
. To proceed, we need the following reminders and preliminary results.

1) According to
Equation 1
, the Mahalanobis norms
\nu_{\bm{C}_{n}}
and
\nu_{{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}}
, respectively associated with the diagonal matrices
\bm{C}_{n}
and
{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}
, are given by

\displaystyle\nu_{\bm{C}_{n}}(\bm{x})=\|\bm{C}_{n}^{-1/2}\bm{x}\,\|\,,

(70a)

\displaystyle\nu_{{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}}(\bm{x})=\|{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1/2}\bm{x}\,\|\,.

(70b)

Since we have set
Equation 54
&
Equation 56
, we also have:

\displaystyle\big(\bm{C}_{n}^{-1/2}\bm{x}\big)_{i}={\sigma_{n,i}^{-1}}\,x_{i}\,,

(71a)

\displaystyle\big({{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1/2}\bm{x}\big)_{i}={\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!n,i}^{\,-1}\,x_{i}\,.

(71b)

2) We derive from
Equation 12
,
Equation 54
&
Equation 56
that:

\begin{array}[]{lll}\forall\,\bm{Y}\in{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}})},\forall\bm{\Phi}\in{\mathbb{S}},\forall\bm{\varphi}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}},\forall n\in\mathbb{N},\\
\hskip 28.45274pt{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1/2}(\bm{Y}({\bm{\Phi},n})-\bm{\varphi})\thicksim\mathcal{N}\left({{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1/2}(\bm{\Phi}(\kappa_{n})-\bm{\varphi}),\operatorname{diag}\left({\dfrac{\sigma^{\,2}_{n,1}}{{\sigma^{\prime}}^{\,2}_{\!\!\!n,1}},\ldots,\dfrac{\sigma^{\,2}_{n,\mathrm{d}}}{{\sigma^{\prime}}^{\,2}_{\!\!\!n,\mathrm{d}}}}\right)\right)\,.\end{array}

(72)

3) It is also convenient to introduce the following definition.

Definition 8
.

Given any interval
[{\lambda_{\text{min}}^{2}},{\lambda_{\text{max}}^{2}}]\subset{]0,\infty[}
,
{\operatorname{GDiag}}{[{\lambda_{\text{min}}^{2}},{\lambda_{\text{max}}^{2}}]}
is the set of all Gaussian-distributed
\bm{X}\in\mathcal{M}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}})
whose covariance matrix is diagonal with diagonal elements in
[{\lambda_{\text{min}}^{2}},{\lambda_{\text{max}}^{2}}]
.

With this definition, it follows from
Equation 55
,
Equation 57
&
Equation 72
that

\begin{array}[]{lll}\forall\,\bm{Y}\in{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}})},\forall\bm{\Phi}\in{\mathbb{S}},\forall\bm{\varphi}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}},\forall n\in\mathbb{N},\\
\hskip 85.35826pt{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1/2}(\bm{Y}({\bm{\Phi},n})-\bm{\varphi})\in{\operatorname{GDiag}}{[{\lambda_{\text{min}}^{2}},{\lambda_{\text{max}}^{2}}]}\,,\end{array}

(73)

where we henceforth put

{\lambda_{\text{min}}^{2}}={\sigma^{\,2}_{\text{min}}\,/\,{\sigma^{\prime}}^{\,2}_{\!\!\text{max}}}\quad\&\quad{\lambda_{\text{max}}^{2}}={\sigma^{\,2}_{\text{max}}\,/\,{\sigma^{\prime}}^{\,2}_{\!\!\text{min}}}\,.

(74)

Note also that, according to
Equation 71b
, we have the next inequality:

\hskip-5.69046pt\forall n\in\mathbb{N},\forall\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}},{{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!\text{max}}^{\,-1}}\|\bm{x}\|\leqslant\|{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1/2}\bm{x}\|\leqslant{{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!\text{min}}^{\,-1}}\|\bm{x}\|\,.

(75)

The next lemma provides approximations of
\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k,N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]
and
\mathbb{E}\big[T_{k,N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]
defined by
Equation 62
, in function of
\|\bm{\varphi}-\bm{\Phi}(k)\|
. This behavior is rewritten in Corollary
3
in function of
D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})
.

Lemma 4
.

For any
\varepsilon\in{]0,\infty[}
, there exists
r\in{]0,\infty[}
such that:

\begin{array}[]{lll}\forall(k,N)\in\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket\times{\llbracket K,\infty\llbracket},\forall i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket,\forall\,\bm{Y}\in{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}})},\\
\hskip 56.9055pt\forall\bm{\Phi}\in{\mathbb{S}},\forall\bm{\varphi}\in\mathcal{B}(\bm{\Phi}(k),r)^{c},\left\{\begin{array}[]{llll}\!\!\!\left|\,\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k,N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\,\right|<\varepsilon\\
\!\!\text{\large$\bm{\wedge}$}\\
\!\!\!\mathbb{E}\big[T_{k,N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]<\varepsilon\,.\end{array}\right.\end{array}

(76)

Proof.

Consider any
\varepsilon\in{]0,\infty[}
. Since
w
is continuous and
\displaystyle\lim\limits_{t\rightarrow\infty}{tw(t)}=0
by
2
, the function
t\in{[0,\infty[}\mapsto tw(t)\in{[0,\infty[}
is bounded. Therefore,
\displaystyle\lim\limits_{t\rightarrow\infty}w(t)=0
as well. We thus apply Lemma
13
of Appendix
B
to the functions
t\mapsto t{w(t^{2})}
and
t\mapsto{w(t^{2})}
to derive the existence of
r^{\prime}\in{]0,\infty[}
such that, for any
\bm{X}\in{\operatorname{GDiag}}{[{\lambda_{\text{min}}^{2}},{\lambda_{\text{max}}^{2}}]}
satisfying
\|\,\mathbb{E}\big[\bm{X}\big]\|>r^{\prime}
,

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\!\!\left\|\mathbb{E}\big[w(\|\bm{X}\|^{2})\bm{X}\big]\right\|\leqslant\mathbb{E}\big[w(\|\bm{X}\|^{2})\|\bm{X}\|\big]<\varepsilon{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!\text{min}}\,,\\
\!\!\mathbb{E}\big[w(\|\bm{X}\|^{2})\big]<{\varepsilon}{\sigma^{\prime}}^{\,2}_{\!\!\text{min}}\,.\end{array}\right.

(77)

where the slant inequality above is standard by Jensen’s inequality applied to
\|\cdot\|
.

Set
r=r^{\prime}\times{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!\text{max}}
and fix arbitrarily
(k,N)\in\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket\times{\llbracket K,\infty\llbracket}
,
\bm{\Phi}\in{\mathbb{S}}
,
\bm{\varphi}\in\mathcal{B}(\bm{\Phi}(k),r)^{c}
,
\bm{Y}\in{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}})}
and
n\in\mathcal{C}_{k,N}
. We deduce from
Equation 60
,
Equation 72
and the first inequality in
Equation 75
that

\hskip-5.69046pt\big\|\mathbb{E}[\,{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1/2}(\bm{Y}({\bm{\Phi},n})-\bm{\varphi})\,]\big\|=\big\|{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1/2}(\bm{\Phi}(k)-\bm{\varphi})\big\|>r^{\prime}

(78)

and thus, from
Equation 73
,
Equation 77
&
Equation 78
that:

\displaystyle\big\|\,\mathbb{E}\big[\,w(\|{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1/2}(\bm{Y}({\bm{\Phi},n})-\bm{\varphi})\|^{2})\,{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1/2}(\bm{Y}({\bm{\Phi},n})-\bm{\varphi})\big]\big\|<\varepsilon{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!\text{min}}\,,

(79a)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\,w(\|{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1/2}(\bm{Y}({\bm{\Phi},n})-\bm{\varphi})\|^{2})\,\big]<{\varepsilon}{\sigma^{\prime}}^{\,2}_{\!\!\text{min}}\,.

(79b)

For any
i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket
,
Equation 71b
induces that

\big({{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1/2}(\bm{Y}({\bm{\Phi},n})-\bm{\varphi})\big)_{i}={\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!n,i}^{\,-1}\big(\bm{Y}({\bm{\Phi},n})-\bm{\varphi}\big)_{i}

and thereby that:

\displaystyle\big|\,\mathbb{E}\,\big[\,w(\|

\displaystyle{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1/2}(\bm{Y}({\bm{\Phi},n})-\bm{\varphi})\|^{2})\,{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!n,i}^{\,-1}\big(\bm{Y}({\bm{\Phi},n})-\bm{\varphi}\big)_{i}\,]\,\big|\vskip 2.84544pt

\displaystyle=\big|\,\mathbb{E}\big[\,w(\|{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1/2}(\bm{Y}({\bm{\Phi},n})-\bm{\varphi})\|^{2})\big({{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1/2}(\bm{Y}({\bm{\Phi},n})-\bm{\varphi})\big)_{i}\,\big]\,\big|\vskip 2.84544pt

\displaystyle\leqslant\big\|\,\mathbb{E}\big[\,w(\|{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1/2}(\bm{Y}({\bm{\Phi},n})-\bm{\varphi})\|^{2})\,{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1/2}(\bm{Y}({\bm{\Phi},n})-\bm{\varphi})\big]\big\|\,.

It results from the inequality above,
Equation 79
and
Equation 57
that, for all
i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket
,

\left\{\hskip-2.84544pt\begin{array}[]{lll}\begin{array}[]{lll}\Big|\,\mathbb{E}\big[\,w\big(\|{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1/2}(\bm{Y}({\bm{\Phi},n})-\bm{\varphi})\|^{2}\,\big)\,{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!n,i}^{\,-2}\,\big(\bm{Y}({\bm{\Phi},n})-\bm{\varphi}\big)_{i}\,\big]\,\Big|\\
\hskip 56.9055pt={\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!n,i}^{\,-1}\big|\,\mathbb{E}\big[\,w\big(\|{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1/2}(\bm{Y}({\bm{\Phi},n})-\bm{\varphi})\|^{2}\,\big)\,{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!n,i}^{\,-1}\,\big(\bm{Y}({\bm{\Phi},n})-\bm{\varphi}\big)_{i}\,\big]\,\big|\\
\hskip 56.9055pt\leqslant{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!n,i}^{\,-1}\,\big\|\,\mathbb{E}\big[\,w(\|{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1/2}(\bm{Y}({\bm{\Phi},n})-\bm{\varphi})\|^{2})\,{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1/2}(\bm{Y}({\bm{\Phi},n})-\bm{\varphi})\big\|\big]\\
\hskip 56.9055pt<{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!n,i}^{\,-1}\,{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!\text{min}}\,\varepsilon\\
\hskip 56.9055pt<\varepsilon\end{array}\\
\mathbb{E}\big[\,w\big(\,\|{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1/2}(\bm{Y}({\bm{\Phi},n})\!-\!\bm{\varphi})\|^{2}\big)\,\big]{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!n,i}^{\,-2}<\varepsilon\,.\end{array}\right.

Whence the result by
Equation 70b
&
Equation 62
, where we take
\bm{W}=\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi})
and
W_{n}=\bm{Y}({\bm{\Phi},n})
.
∎

For what follows, we recall that
{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}
,
{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}})}
, and
D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})
, are defined by Definitions
4
,
5
, and
6
, respectively.

Corollary 3
.

For any
\varepsilon\in{]0,\infty[}
and any pair
(\bm{\varphi}_{0},\bm{\varphi})\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}\times\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
, there exists
\rho\in{]0,\infty[}
such that

\begin{array}[]{lll}\forall i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket,\forall k_{0}\in\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket,\forall\,\bm{Y}\in{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}})},\forall\bm{\Phi}\in{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},\\
\hskip 28.45274ptD^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})>\rho\Rightarrow\forall(k,N)\in\big(\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket\setminus\{k_{0}\}\big)\times{\llbracket K,\infty\llbracket},\left\{\begin{array}[]{llll}\hskip-5.69046pt\left|\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k,N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\right|<\varepsilon\\
\text{\large$\bm{\wedge}$}\\
\hskip-4.26773pt\mathbb{E}\big[T_{k,N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]<\varepsilon\,.\end{array}\right.\end{array}

Proof.

Let
\varepsilon\in{]0,\infty[}
. Lemma
4
implies the existence of
r\in{]0,\infty[}
such that
Equation 76
holds true. Given any
(\bm{\varphi}_{0},\bm{\varphi})\!\in\!\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}\times\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
, set
\rho=r+\|\bm{\varphi}-\bm{\varphi}_{0}\|
. Consider arbitrary
k_{0}\in\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket
,
\bm{Y}\in{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}})}
and
\bm{\Phi}\in{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}
. If
D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})>\rho
then, for any
k\in\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket\setminus\{k_{0}\}
,
\|\bm{\Phi}(k)-\bm{\varphi}_{0}\|>\rho
, which implies that
\|\bm{\Phi}(k)-\bm{\varphi}\|\geqslant\|\bm{\Phi}(k)-\bm{\varphi}_{0}\|-\|\bm{\varphi}-\bm{\varphi}_{0}\|>r
. Thence the result as a consequence of
Equation 76
.
∎

For
\bm{\varphi}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
,
N\in{\llbracket K,\infty\llbracket}
,
\bm{Y}\in{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}})}
and
\bm{\Phi}\in{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}
, it follows from
Equation 63
that

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\!\!\mathbb{E}\big[\bm{U}_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]=\sum_{k=1}^{K}a_{k,N}\,\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k,N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\,,\\
\!\!\mathbb{E}\big[V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]=\sum_{k=1}^{K}a_{k,N}\,\mathbb{E}\big[T_{k,N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\,.\end{array}\right.

According to Lemma
3
and Corollary
3
, the equalities above suggest that, when
N
and
D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})
are both large enough,
\bm{U}_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))
and
V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))
can be approximated by
a_{k_{0},N}\mathbb{E}\big[\bm{S}_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]
and
a_{k_{0},N}\mathbb{E}\big[T_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]
, respectively. This heuristic is formalized by Corollary
4
below, according to which

\dfrac{\bm{U}_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))-a_{k_{0},N}\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]}{V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))}

vanishes when
N
and
D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})
tend to
\infty
. To establish Lemma
6
, the next result will be instrumental. In this lemma and the rest of the proof, we set

\begin{array}[]{lll}\Psi(r)=\dfrac{1}{(2\pi)^{\mathrm{d}/2}{\lambda_{\text{max}}^{\mathrm{d}}}}\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}}w\left(\left(\|\bm{x}\|+r\right)^{2}\right)e^{-\|\bm{x}\|^{2}/2{\lambda_{\text{min}}^{2}}}\,d\bm{x}\,.\end{array}

(80)

Note that
\Psi
does not increase since
w
does not.

Lemma 5
.

Given
\bm{Y}\in{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}})}
,
\bm{\Phi}\in{\mathbb{S}}
,
r\in{[0,\infty[}
,
\bm{\varphi}\in\mathcal{B}(\bm{\Phi}(k),r)
,
(k,N)\in\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket\times{\llbracket K,\infty\llbracket}
and
i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket
,

\mathbb{E}\big[T_{k,N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\geqslant\Psi\Big({\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!\text{min}}^{\,-1}r\Big)\,{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!\text{max}}^{\,-2}>0\,.

(81)

Proof.

Fix arbitrarily
\bm{Y}\in{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}})}
,
\bm{\Phi}\in{\mathbb{S}}
,
(k,N)\in\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket\times{\llbracket K,\infty\llbracket}
,
n\in\mathcal{C}_{k,N}
. For any
\bm{\varphi}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
, it results from
Equation 60
&
Equation 72
, that

\begin{array}[]{lll}\mathbb{E}\big[w\big(\|{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1/2}(\bm{Y}({\bm{\Phi},n})-\bm{\varphi})\|^{2}\big)\big]\\
\hskip 28.45274pt=\dfrac{1}{(2\pi)^{\mathrm{d}/2}\,\prod_{i=1}^{\mathrm{d}}{\lambda_{n,i}}}\displaystyle\int w\big(\,\|\bm{x}+{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1/2}(\bm{\Phi}(k)\!-\!\bm{\varphi})\|^{2}\,\big)\,e^{-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{\mathrm{d}}x_{i}^{2}/{\lambda^{2}_{n,i}}}\,d\bm{x}\end{array}

(82)

with
\bm{x}=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{\mathrm{d}})^{\mathrm{T}}
and
{\lambda^{2}_{n,i}=\sigma^{\,2}_{n,i}\,/\,{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!n,i}^{\,2}}
for all
i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket
. Because of
Equation 73
, we have
\prod_{i=1}^{\mathrm{d}}\lambda_{n,i}\leqslant\lambda_{\text{max}}^{\mathrm{d}}
and, for all
\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
,
e^{-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{\mathrm{d}}x_{i}^{2}/\lambda^{2}_{n,i}}\geqslant e^{-\frac{1}{2}\|\bm{x}\|^{2}/\lambda_{\text{min}}^{2}}
.
In addition, the triangle inequality and the non-increasingness of
w
induce that, for all
\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
,

\forall\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}},w\big(\,\|\bm{x}+{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1/2}(\bm{\Phi}(k)-\bm{\varphi})\|^{2}\,\big)\geqslant w\left(\big(\,\|\bm{x}\|+\|{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1/2}(\bm{\Phi}(k)-\bm{\varphi}\big)\|\,)^{2}\right)\,.

Injecting the three inequalities above into
Equation 82
yields

\mathbb{E}\big[w\big(\|{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1/2}(\bm{Y}({\bm{\Phi},n})-\bm{\varphi})\|^{2}\big)\big]\geqslant\Psi\left(\|{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1/2}(\bm{\Phi}(k)-\bm{\varphi})\|\right)\,.

(83)

Take any
\bm{\varphi}\in\mathcal{B}(\bm{\Phi}(k),r)
. The second inequality in
Equation 75
implies that

\|{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1/2}(\bm{\Phi}(k)-\bm{\varphi})\|\leqslant{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!\text{min}}^{\,-1}r\,.

Since
\Psi
does not increase, we derive from the inequality above and
Equation 83
that

\mathbb{E}\big[w\big(\|{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1/2}(\bm{Y}({\bm{\Phi},n})-\bm{\varphi})\|^{2}\big)\big]\geqslant\Psi\Big({{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!\text{min}}^{\,-1}}r\Big)\,.

(84)

From
Equation 62b
with
\bm{W}=\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi})
and
\bm{W}_{n}=\bm{Y}({\bm{\Phi},n})
, we have

\mathbb{E}\big[T_{k,N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]=\dfrac{1}{\operatorname{card}\!\left(\mathcal{C}_{k,N}\right)}\hskip-8.5359pt\displaystyle\sum_{\hskip 8.19447ptn\in\mathcal{C}_{k,N}}\hskip-5.69046pt\mathbb{E}\big[w\big(\nu_{{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}(\bm{Y}({\bm{\Phi},n})-\bm{\varphi})\big)\big]\,{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!n,i}^{\,-2}

for any
i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket
. The slack inequality in
Equation 81
follows by injecting
Equation 70b
and
Equation 84
into the equality above and taking that
{{\sigma^{\prime}}_{n,i}^{-2}}\geqslant{{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\text{max}}^{-2}}
for all
i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket
into account. The strict inequality in
Equation 81
results from the definition of
\Psi
.
∎

Lemma 6
.

Given
i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket
,
k_{0}\in\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket
,
(\bm{\varphi}_{0},\bm{\varphi})\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}\times\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
,
\bm{Y}\in{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}})}
and
\eta\in{]0,\infty[}
, there exists
\rho\in{]0,\infty[}
such that, for all
\bm{\Phi}\in{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}
:

\hskip-85.35826pt\begin{array}[]{lll}\hskip-28.45274ptD^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})>\rho\Rightarrow\exists\,\Omega_{0}\in\mathcal{A},\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{0})=1,\forall\omega\in\Omega_{0},\exists N_{0}\in\mathbb{N},\forall N\in\mathbb{N},\end{array}\vskip-14.22636pt

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lll}\hskip-5.69046pt\left|\bm{U}_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))(\omega)\!-\!a_{k_{0},N}\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\right|\!<\!\dfrac{\eta}{1+\eta}\,a_{0}\tau(\|\bm{\varphi}-\bm{\varphi}_{0}\|)\end{array}\hskip-22.76228pt\vskip 2.84544pt

\bm{\wedge}

\displaystyle V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))(\omega)>\dfrac{a_{0}\,\tau(\|\bm{\varphi}-\bm{\varphi}_{0}\|)}{1+\eta}>0

(85b)

where

\forall r\in{[0,\infty[},\tau(r)=\Psi\big({{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!\text{min}}^{\,-1}}\,r\big){{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!\text{max}}^{\,-2}}>0\,.

(86)

Proof.

The proof proceeds by combining predicates drawn from the lemmas above. From now on, we fix arbitrarily
i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket
,
k_{0}\in\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket
,
(\bm{\varphi}_{0},\bm{\varphi})\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}\times\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
and
\eta\in{]0,\infty[}
.

First,
Equation 63
induces, with
\bm{W}=\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi})
, that

Predicate 1
.

\begin{array}[]{l}\begin{array}[]{llll}\forall\,\bm{Y}\in{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}})},\forall\,\bm{\Phi}\in{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},\forall\omega\in\Omega,\forall N\in\mathbb{N},N\geqslant K\implies\\
\hskip 11.38092pt\left\{\begin{array}[]{llll}\hskip-8.5359pt\begin{array}[]{lll}\!\big|\,\bm{U}_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))(\omega)-a_{k_{0},N}\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\big|\\
\begin{array}[]{lll}\leqslant\big|\bm{U}_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))(\omega)-\mathbb{E}\big[\bm{U}_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\big|+\sum_{k=1,k\neq k_{0}}^{K}a_{k,N}\big|\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k,N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\big|\end{array}\end{array}\\
\hskip-2.84544pt\text{\large$\bm{\wedge}$}\\
\hskip-8.5359pt\begin{array}[]{lll}\!\left|V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))(\omega)-a_{k_{0},N}\mathbb{E}\big[T_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\right|\\
\begin{array}[]{lll}\leqslant\big|V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))(\omega)-\mathbb{E}\big[V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\big|+\sum_{k=1,k\neq k_{0}}^{K}a_{k,N}\Big|\mathbb{E}\big[T_{k,N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\Big|\,.\end{array}\end{array}\end{array}\right.\end{array}\end{array}

Setting

\varepsilon=\dfrac{1}{2}\dfrac{\eta}{1+\eta}\,a_{0}\,\tau(\|\bm{\varphi}-\bm{\varphi}_{0}\|),

(87)

Lemma
3
induces that:

Predicate 2
.

\begin{array}[]{l}\begin{array}[]{lll}\forall\,\bm{Y}\in{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}})},\forall\,\bm{\Phi}\in{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},\exists\,\Omega_{0}\in\mathcal{A},\\
\hskip 7.11317pt\left\{\begin{array}[]{llll}\!\!\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{0})=1,\\
\!\!\text{\large$\bm{\wedge}$}\\
\!\!\forall\omega\!\in\!\Omega_{0},\exists N^{\prime}_{0}\!\in\!\mathbb{N},\forall N\!\in\!\mathbb{N},N\!>\!N^{\prime}_{0}\Rightarrow\!\left\{\!\!\!\begin{array}[]{llll}\begin{array}[]{lll}\!\!\big|\,\bm{U}_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))(\omega)-\mathbb{E}\big[\bm{U}_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\big|<\varepsilon\end{array}\\
\text{\large$\bm{\wedge}$}\\
\begin{array}[]{lll}\!\!\big|\,V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))(\omega)-\mathbb{E}\big[V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\big|<\varepsilon\end{array}\end{array}\right.\end{array}\right.\end{array}\end{array}

By combining
1
and
2
, where we take
N_{0}=\max(N^{\prime}_{0},K)
, we obtain:

Predicate 3
.

\begin{array}[]{l}\forall\,\bm{Y}\in{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}})},\forall\,\bm{\Phi}\in{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},\exists\,\Omega_{0}\in\mathcal{A},\\
\hskip 11.38092pt\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\hskip-2.84544pt\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{0})=1\\
\text{\large$\bm{\wedge}$}\\
\hskip-2.84544pt\forall\omega\in\Omega_{0},\exists N_{0}\in\mathbb{N},\forall N\in\mathbb{N},\\
\hskip 8.5359ptN>N_{0}\Rightarrow\hskip-5.69046pt\begin{array}[]{llll}\left\{\begin{array}[]{llll}\begin{array}[]{lll}\hskip-8.5359pt\big|\,\bm{U}_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))(\omega)\!-\!a_{k_{0},N}\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\big|\\
\hskip 56.9055pt<\varepsilon+\!\sum_{k=1,k\neq k_{0}}^{K}a_{k,N}\,\Big|\,\mathbb{E}\big[\bm{S}_{k,N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\,\Big|\\
\hskip-8.5359pt\text{\large$\bm{\wedge}$}\\
\hskip-8.5359pt\big|\,V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))(\omega)-a_{k_{0},N}\mathbb{E}\big[T_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\,\big|\\
\hskip 56.9055pt<\varepsilon+\!\sum_{k=1,k\neq k_{0}}^{K}a_{k,N}\Big|\,\mathbb{E}\big[T_{k,N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\,\Big|\,.\end{array}\end{array}\right.\end{array}\end{array}\right.\end{array}

On the other hand, it follows from Corollary
3
that

Predicate 4
.

\begin{array}[]{l}\begin{array}[]{llll}\exists\,\rho\in{]0,\infty[},\forall\,\bm{Y}\in{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}})},\forall\,\bm{\Phi}\in{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},\\
\hskip 2.84544pt\begin{array}[]{lll}D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})>\rho\Rightarrow\forall(k,N)\in\left(\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket\setminus\{k_{0}\}\right)\times{\llbracket K,\infty\llbracket},\left\{\!\!\begin{array}[]{llll}\big|\,\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k,N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\,\big|<\varepsilon\\
\text{\large$\bm{\wedge}$}\\
\mathbb{E}\big[T_{k,N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]<\varepsilon\,.\end{array}\right.\end{array}\end{array}\end{array}

It results from
3
and
4
and the definition of the coefficients
a_{k,N}
given by
Equation 61
that:

Predicate 5
.

\hskip-28.45274pt\begin{array}[]{l}\begin{array}[]{llll}\exists\,\rho\in{]0,\infty[},\forall\,\bm{Y}\in{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}})},\forall\,\bm{\Phi}\in{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},\exists\,\Omega_{0}\in\mathcal{A},\\
\hskip 14.22636pt\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\!\!\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{0})=1\\
\!\!\text{\large$\bm{\wedge}$}\\
\!\!\forall\,\omega\in\Omega_{0},\exists N_{0}\in\mathbb{N},\forall N\in\mathbb{N},\\
\hskip 28.45274pt\big(N>N_{0}\big)\wedge\big(D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})>\rho\big)\implies\\
\hskip 64.01869pt\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\hskip-9.95863pt\begin{array}[]{lll}\begin{array}[]{lll}\hskip-2.84544pt\big|\,\bm{U}_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))(\omega)-a_{k_{0},N}\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\big|<2\varepsilon\end{array}\end{array}\\
\text{\large$\bm{\wedge}$}\\
\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip-9.95863pt\begin{array}[]{lll}\hskip-2.84544pt\big|\,V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))(\omega)-a_{k_{0},N}\mathbb{E}\big[T_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\big|<2\varepsilon\,.\end{array}\end{array}\end{array}\right.\end{array}\right.\end{array}\end{array}

On the one hand,
D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})
is unequivocally determined by
\bm{\Phi}
,
k_{0}
and
\bm{\varphi}_{0}
. On the other hand, according to Corollary
3
and the definition of
\varepsilon
given by
Equation 87
,
\rho
depends on
\eta,\bm{\varphi}_{0},\bm{\varphi}
only. Therefore,
5
implies

Predicate 6
.

\begin{array}[]{l}\begin{array}[]{llll}\hskip-9.95863pt\forall\,\bm{Y}\in{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}})},\exists\,\rho\in{]0,\infty[},\forall\,\bm{\Phi}\in{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},\\
D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})>\rho\Rightarrow\exists\,\Omega_{0}\in\mathcal{A},\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\!\!\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{0})=1\\
\!\!\text{\large$\bm{\wedge}$}\\
\!\!\forall\omega\in\Omega_{0},\exists N_{0}\in\mathbb{N},\forall N\in\mathbb{N},N>N_{0}\Rightarrow\\
\hskip 2.84544pt\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\hskip-9.95863pt\begin{array}[]{lll}\begin{array}[]{lll}\hskip-4.26773pt\big|\,\bm{U}_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))(\omega)-a_{k_{0},N}\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\,\big|<2\varepsilon\end{array}\end{array}\\
\text{\large$\bm{\wedge}$}\\
\hskip-4.26773ptV_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))(\omega)>a_{k_{0},N}\mathbb{E}\big[T_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]-2\varepsilon\,.\end{array}\right.\end{array}\right.\end{array}\end{array}

We have

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}a_{k_{0},N}\geqslant a_{0}&\text{[from \autoref{eq: inequality on alpha}]}\,,\\
a_{0}\,\tau(\|\bm{\varphi}-\bm{\varphi}_{0}\|)-2\varepsilon=\dfrac{a_{0}\,\tau(\|\bm{\varphi}-\bm{\varphi}_{0}\|)}{1+\eta}>0&\text{[from \autoref{eq: definition of varepsilon}]}\,.\end{array}\right.

(88)

On the other hand, given
\bm{Y}\in{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}})}
and
\bm{\Phi}\in{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}
, for which we thus have
\bm{\Phi}(k_{0})=\bm{\varphi}_{0}
, we derive from
Equation 86
and Lemma
5
, with
r=\|\bm{\varphi}-\bm{\varphi}_{0}\|
, that:

\forall N\in{\llbracket K,\infty\llbracket},\mathbb{E}\big[T_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\geqslant\tau(\|\bm{\varphi}-\bm{\varphi}_{0}\|)>0\,.

(89)

The result thus follows by injecting
Equation 87
,
Equation 88
and
Equation 89
into
6
.
∎

Corollary 4

(of Lemma
6
)
.

Given
i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket
,
k_{0}\in\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket
,
(\bm{\varphi}_{0},\bm{\varphi})\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}\times\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
and
\bm{Y}\in{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}})}
,

\begin{array}[]{lll}\displaystyle\mathop{\text{ess. lim. sup}}_{D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})\to\infty\,,\,N\to\infty}\Big(\bm{U}_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))-a_{k_{0},N}\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\Big)\\
\hskip 28.45274pt=\displaystyle\mathop{\text{ess. lim. sup}}_{D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})\to\infty\,,\,N\to\infty}\dfrac{\bm{U}_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))-a_{k_{0},N}\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]}{V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))}\\
\hskip 28.45274pt=0\,.\end{array}

Proof.

Pick any
i\!\in\!\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket
,
k_{0}\!\in\!\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket
,
(\bm{\varphi}_{0},\bm{\varphi})\!\in\!\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}\!\times\!\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
, and
\bm{Y}\!\in\!{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}})}
.
For any
(\bm{\Phi},N)\in{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}\times{\llbracket K,\infty\llbracket}
, define
\bm{F}_{1}(\bm{\Phi},N)
by

\bm{F}_{1}(\bm{\Phi},N)=\bm{U}_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))-a_{k_{0},N}\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\,.

Consider any
\eta^{\prime}\in{]0,a_{0}\tau(\|\bm{\varphi}-\bm{\varphi}_{0}\|)[}
, with
\tau(\|\bm{\varphi}-\bm{\varphi}_{0}\|))
given by
Equation 86
, and set

\eta=\frac{\eta^{\prime}}{a_{0}\tau(\|\bm{\varphi}-\bm{\varphi}_{0}\|)-\eta^{\prime}}\,.

From
Equation 85
in Lemma
6
, we obtain that

\hskip-17.07182pt\begin{array}[]{lll}\exists\,\rho\in{]0,\infty[},\forall\,\bm{\Phi}\in{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})>\rho\,\Rightarrow\,\exists\,\Omega_{0}\in\mathcal{A},\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{0})=1,\forall\omega\in\Omega_{0},\exists N_{0}\in\mathbb{N},\\
\hskip 213.39566pt{\forall N\in\mathbb{N},N>N_{0}\,\Rightarrow}\,|\bm{F}_{1}(\bm{\Phi},N)(\omega)|<\eta^{\prime}\,.\end{array}\hskip-8.5359pt

(90)

Therefore, according to Corollary
2
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathop{\text{ess. lim. sup}}_{D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})\to\infty\,,\,N\to\infty}\bm{F}_{1}(\bm{\Phi},N)=0

and thus, the first equality.

Now, given any
(\bm{\Phi},N)\in{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}\times{\llbracket K,\infty\llbracket}
, set

\bm{F}_{2}(\bm{\Phi},N)=\dfrac{\bm{U}_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))-a_{k_{0},N}\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]}{V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))}\,.

This parametric discrete random process is well defined because
Equation 62b
,
Equation 63b
and the fact that
w(t)>0
for all
t\in{[0,\infty[}
by
2
, imply that
V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))>0
. Given any
\eta\in{]0,\infty[}
, we derive from
Equation 85
that

\hskip-9.95863pt\begin{array}[]{lll}\exists\,\rho\in{]0,\infty[},\forall\,\bm{\Phi}\in{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})>\rho\Rightarrow\\
\hskip 14.22636pt\exists\,\Omega_{0}\in\mathcal{A},\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{0})=1,\forall\,\omega\in\Omega_{0},\exists N_{0}\in\mathbb{N},\forall N\in\mathbb{N},N>N_{0}\Rightarrow|\,\bm{F}_{2}(\bm{\Phi},N)(\omega)\,|<\eta\,.\end{array}\hskip-8.5359pt

(91)

Therefore, according to Corollary
2
,

\displaystyle\mathop{\text{ess. lim. sup}}_{D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})\to\infty\,,\,N\to\infty}\bm{F}_{2}(\bm{\Phi},N)=0\,.

Whence the second equality.
∎

For further use below, note that, given
k_{0}\!\in\!\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket
,
N\!\in\!{\llbracket K,\infty\llbracket}
,
i\!\in\!\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket
,
\bm{\varphi}\!\in\!\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
,
\bm{Y}\!\in\!{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}})}
and
\bm{\Phi}\!\in\!{\mathbb{S}}
, it results from
Equation 62a
,
Equation 70b
&
Equation 71b
that:

\hskip-2.84544ptS_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\!=\!\dfrac{1}{\operatorname{card}\!\left(\mathcal{C}_{k_{0},N}\right)}\!\!\displaystyle\sum_{n\in\mathcal{C}_{k_{0},N}}\hskip-7.11317pt\!w\Big(\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\mathrm{d}}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!n,j}^{\,-2}(\bm{Y}({\bm{\Phi},n})-\bm{\varphi})_{j}^{2}\,\Big)\,{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!n,i}^{\,-2}\,(\bm{Y}({\bm{\Phi},n})-\bm{\varphi})_{i}\,.

(92)

Also, if
k_{0}\!\in\!\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket
,
N\!\in\!{\llbracket K,\infty\llbracket}
,
i\!\in\!\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket
,
(\bm{\varphi}_{0},\bm{\varphi})\!\in\!\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}\times\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
,
\bm{Y}\!\in\!{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}})}
and
\bm{\Phi}\!\in\!{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}
, then
\bm{\Phi}(\kappa_{n})\!=\!\bm{\Phi}(k_{0})\!=\!\bm{\varphi}_{0}
for all
n\!\in\!\mathcal{C}_{k_{0},N}
and thus, according to
Equation 12
and
Equation 54
:

\forall\,n\in\mathcal{C}_{k_{0},N},\forall\,i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket,({\bm{Y}\!\left({\bm{\Phi},n}\right)}-\bm{\varphi})_{i}\thicksim\mathcal{N}\left((\varphi_{0}-\varphi)_{i},\sigma^{\,2}_{\!n,i}\right)\,.

(93)

Lemma 7
.

Given
k_{0}\in\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket
,
(\bm{\varphi}_{0},\bm{\varphi})\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}\times\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
,
\bm{Y}\in{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}})}
and
\bm{\Phi}\in{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}
,

\left(\,\forall\,i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket,\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]=0\,\right)\,\,\,\Leftrightarrow\,\,\,\bm{\varphi}=\bm{\varphi}_{0}

Proof.

Fix arbitrarily
k_{0}\in\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket
,
(\bm{\varphi}_{0},\bm{\varphi})\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}\times\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
,
\bm{Y}\in{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}})}
,
\bm{\Phi}\in{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}
and
i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket
. Let
N\in{\llbracket K,\infty\llbracket}
. Set
M=\operatorname{card}\!\left(\mathcal{C}_{k_{0},N}\right)
and write
\mathcal{C}_{k_{0},N}=\{n_{1},n_{2},\ldots,n_{M}\}
by ordering the elements of this set. For each
m\in\llbracket 1,M\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket
and each
i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket
, put

X_{m,i}=\sigma^{-1}_{n_{m},i}\left(\bm{Y}({\bm{\Phi},n_{m}})-\bm{\varphi}\right)_{i}\,.

We thus derive from
Equation 93
that

X_{m,i}\thicksim\mathcal{N}\left(\sigma^{-1}_{n_{m},i}(\varphi_{0}-\varphi)_{i},1\right)\,.

(94)

If we set
\bm{X}_{m}=(X_{m,1},\ldots,X_{m,\mathrm{d}})^{\mathrm{T}}
, we thus have

\forall m\in\llbracket 1,M\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket,\bm{X}_{m}\thicksim\mathcal{N}(\bm{\Lambda}_{m}\bm{\theta},\bm{I}_{{\mathrm{d}}})\,\text{with}\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\hskip-2.84544pt\bm{\Lambda}_{m}=\bm{C}_{n_{m}}^{\,-1/2}\,,\\
\hskip-2.84544pt\bm{\theta}=\bm{\varphi}_{0}-\bm{\varphi}\,.\end{array}\right.\hskip-11.38092pt

(95)

Thence, according to
Equation 92
,
S_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))
rewrites as

S_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))=\dfrac{1}{M}\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{M}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!n_{m},i}^{\,-2}\,\sigma_{n_{m},i}\,w\Big(\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\mathrm{d}}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!n_{m},j}^{\,-2}\,\sigma_{n_{m},j}^{2}\,X_{m,j}^{2}\,\Big)\,X_{m,i}\,.

Therefore, we have:

\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]=\dfrac{1}{M}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\mathbb{E}\big[\bm{f}_{m}(\bm{X}_{m})X_{m,i}\big]

(96)

with

\forall\,\bm{x}=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{\mathrm{d}})^{\mathrm{T}}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}},\bm{f}_{m}(\bm{x})={\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!n_{m},i}^{\,-2}\,\sigma_{n_{m},i}\,w\Big(\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\mathrm{d}}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!n_{m},j}^{\,-2}\,\sigma_{n_{m},j}^{\,2}\,x_{j}^{\,2}\,\Big)\,.

Since
1
induces the equivalence between an arbitrarily large
N
and an arbitrarily large
M
, it follows from
Equation 96
that

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]=0\,\Leftrightarrow\,\displaystyle\lim_{M\to\infty}\dfrac{1}{M}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\mathbb{E}\big[\bm{f}_{m}(\bm{X}_{m})X_{m,i}\big]=0\,.

(97)

According to
Equation 54
and
Equation 55
, for all
m\in\llbracket 1,M\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket
,
\bm{\Lambda}_{m}
is diagonal with diagonal elements in
[\sigma_{\text{max}}^{\,-1},\sigma_{\text{min}}^{\,-1}]\subset{]0,\infty[}
. In addition,
w(t)
does not increase with
t
and it follows from
2
that
w(t)
is finitely upper-bounded. Without loss of generality, suppose that this upper-bound is
1
. Therefore, it follows from
subsection A.2
that, for any
m\in\llbracket 1,M\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket
,
0<\bm{f}^{*}\leqslant\bm{f}_{m}\leqslant{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!\text{min}}^{-2}\,\sigma_{\text{max}}
with

\forall\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}},\bm{f}^{*}(\bm{x})={\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!\text{max}}^{\,-2}\,\sigma_{\text{min}}\,w\left({\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!\text{min}}^{\,-2}\,\sigma_{\text{max}}^{\,2}\,\|\bm{x}\|^{2}\right)\,.

For any
m\in\llbracket 1,M\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket
,
\bm{f}_{m}(\bm{x})
is, in the sense of Definition
9
in Appendix
B
, even in each coordinate of
\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
. The assumptions of Lemma
11
are thus satisfied and the result follows from
Equation 95
,
Equation 96
,
Equation 97
&
Equation 123
in Lemma
11
.
∎

We now achieve the proof of
(\textbf{A})
in Theorem
1
for diagonal positive definite matrices. As of now, we fix arbitrarily
k_{0}\in\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket
,
(\bm{\varphi}_{0},\bm{\varphi})\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}\times\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
and
\bm{Y}\in{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}})}
.

We begin with the direct implication. Suppose that
\bm{\varphi}=\bm{\varphi}_{0}
. Consider any
\bm{\Phi}\in{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}
and any
i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket
. Lemma
7
implies that

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]=0\,.

We thus have:

\forall\,\varepsilon\in{]0,a_{0}\tau(0)[},\exists\,N^{\prime}_{0}\in\mathbb{N},{\forall N\,\in\mathbb{N},N>N^{\prime}_{0}\Rightarrow}\,0\leqslant\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]<\varepsilon^{2}\,.

(98)

On the other hand, set
\eta=\frac{a_{0}\tau(0)-\varepsilon}{\varepsilon}
. Since
\eta\in{]0,\infty[}
, it follows from
Equation 85b
in Lemma
6
and our choice for
\eta
that:

\hskip-5.69046pt\begin{array}[]{lll}\exists\,\rho\in{]0,\infty[},\forall\,\bm{\Phi}\in{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},\\
\hskip 25.6073ptD^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})>\rho\Rightarrow\exists\,\Omega_{0}\in\mathcal{A},\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\hskip-2.84544pt\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{0})=1,\forall\omega\in\Omega_{0},\exists N^{\prime\prime}_{0}\in\mathbb{N},\\
\hskip-2.84544pt\forall N\in\mathbb{N},N>N^{\prime\prime}_{0}\,\Rightarrow\,\,V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))(\omega)>\varepsilon>0\,.\end{array}\right.\end{array}\hskip-2.84544pt

(99)

Define
\bm{F}(\bm{\Phi},N)
for any
(\bm{\Phi},N)\in{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}\times\mathbb{N}
by setting

\bm{F}(\bm{\Phi},N)=\dfrac{\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]}{V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))}\,.

By combining
Equation 98
&
Equation 99
, setting
N_{0}=\max\{N^{\prime}_{0},N^{\prime\prime}_{0}\}
and taking into account that, by definition of
a_{k_{0},N}
given in
Equation 61
,
a_{k_{0},N}\leqslant 1
for all
N\in\mathbb{N}
, we obtain:

\begin{array}[]{lll}\forall\,\varepsilon\in{]0,a_{0}\tau(0)[},\exists\,\rho\in{]0,\infty[},\forall\,\bm{\Phi}\in{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},\\
\hskip 42.67912ptD^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})>\rho\Rightarrow\exists\,\Omega_{0}\in\mathcal{A},\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{0})=1,\forall\omega\in\Omega_{0},\exists N_{0}\in\mathbb{N},\\
\hskip 142.26378pt{\forall N\in\mathbb{N},N>N_{0}\Rightarrow}\,0\leqslant a_{k_{0},N}\bm{F}(\bm{\Phi},N)(\omega)<\varepsilon\,.\end{array}\hskip-14.22636pt

It follows from the predicate above and Corollary
2
of Proposition
2
that

\displaystyle\mathop{\text{ess. lim. sup}}_{D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})\to\infty\,,\,N\to\infty}a_{k_{0},N}\bm{F}(\bm{\Phi},N)=0\,.

Since
i
is arbitrary in
\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket
, we thus have

\forall i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket,\displaystyle\mathop{\text{ess. lim. sup}}_{D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})\to\infty\,,\,N\to\infty}\dfrac{a_{k_{0},N}\,\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]}{V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))}=0\,.

(100)

Consequently, by Proposition
5
and the second equality in Corollary
4
of Lemma
6
,
Equation 100
is equivalent to

\forall i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket,\displaystyle\mathop{\text{ess. lim. sup}}_{D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})\to\infty\,,\,N\to\infty}\,\dfrac{\bm{U}_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))}{V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))}=0\,.

(101)

We thus derive from
Equation 59
and
Equation 25
that
Equation 101
is equivalent to

\displaystyle\mathop{\text{ess. lim. sup}}_{D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})\to\infty\,,\,N\to\infty}\bm{G}_{N}^{{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))=\bm{\varphi}\,.

(102)

Conversely, suppose that
Equation 102
holds true. By the equivalences established above, we thus have
Equation 100
. By assumption,
w
is upper-bounded and non-negative. Without loss of generality, suppose that this upper-bound is
1
as in the proof of Lemma
7
. Therefore, according to
Equation 58
and since
w>0
by assumption
1
1

1

Note that
w>0
almost everywhere with respect to Lebesgue measure would suffice.

\forall\omega\in\Omega,0<V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))(\omega)\leqslant{{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!\text{min}}^{\,-2}}\,.

As a consequence, since
a_{k_{0},N}\geqslant a_{0}
by
Equation 61
, we derive from the foregoing that

\forall\omega\in\Omega,\left|\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\right|\leqslant\dfrac{{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!\text{min}}^{\,-2}}{a_{0}}\dfrac{a_{k_{0},N}\left|\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\right|}{V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))(\omega)}

and thus, that

\forall\omega\in\Omega,\displaystyle\underset{N\to\infty}{\overline{\lim}}\left|\,\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\,\right|\leqslant\dfrac{{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!\text{min}}^{\,-2}}{a_{0}}\displaystyle\underset{N\to\infty}{\overline{\lim}}\dfrac{a_{k_{0},N}\left|\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\right|}{V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))(\omega)}\,.

(103)

According to
Equation 47
, there exists
\Omega_{0}\in\mathcal{A}
with
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{0})=1
such that

\forall\omega\in\Omega_{0},\underset{N\to\infty}{\overline{\lim}}\dfrac{a_{k_{0},N}\left|\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\right|}{V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))(\omega)}\leqslant\left|\underset{N\to\infty}{\overline{\lim}}\dfrac{a_{k_{0},N}\left|\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\right|}{V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))}\right|_{\infty}\,.

(104)

Thence,
Equation 103
and
Equation 104
imply that

\underset{N\to\infty}{\overline{\lim}}\left|\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\right|\leqslant\dfrac{{{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!\text{min}}^{\,-2}}}{a_{0}}\left|\underset{N\to\infty}{\overline{\lim}}\dfrac{a_{k_{0},N}\left|\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\right|}{V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))}\right|_{\infty}\,.

(105)

On the other hand, from
Equation 100
and
Equation 26
, we have

\lim_{D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})\to\infty}\left|\underset{N\to\infty}{\overline{\lim}}\dfrac{a_{k_{0},N}\left|\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\right|}{V_{N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))}\right|_{\infty}=0\,.

It thus follows from
Equation 105
that

\lim_{D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})\to\infty}\,\left(\,\underset{N\to\infty}{\overline{\lim}}\left|\,\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\,\right|\,\right)=0\,.

(106)

Since
\bm{Y}\in{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}})}
and
\bm{\Phi}\in{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}
,
Equation 92
and
Equation 93
induce that
\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]
depends only on
\bm{\varphi}_{0}-\bm{\varphi}
,
\bm{C}_{n}
and
{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}
with
n\in\mathcal{C}_{k_{0},N}
. Hence, the value of
\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]
does not depend on
\bm{\Phi}
and thus on

defined by
Equation 16
.
As a consequence, the value of

, which always exists in
[0,\infty]
, does not depend on
D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})
either. Thereby,
Equation 106
implies that:

\underset{N\to\infty}{\overline{\lim}}\left|\,\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\,\right|=\displaystyle\lim_{D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})\to\infty}\left(\,\underset{N\to\infty}{\overline{\lim}}\left|\,\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]\,\right|\,\right)=0\,.

(107)

Therefore,
\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[S_{k_{0},N,i}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))\big]=0
and since
i
is arbitrary in
\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket
, Lemma
7
implies that
\bm{\varphi}=\bm{\varphi}_{0}
.

A.3
Proof of Equivalence (
A
) for non-diagonal symmetric positive-definite matrices

We begin with a preliminary lemma that makes it possible to move from the diagonal case addressed above to the general case of non-diagonal symmetric positive-definite matrices.

Lemma 8
.

If
\bm{C}=(\bm{C}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and
{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}=({{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
are two sequences of symmetric positive-definite matrices satisfying
3
and
4
, then, for any sequence
\bm{W}=(\bm{W}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
of elements of
\mathcal{M}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}})
and any
\bm{\varphi}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
,

\bm{G}_{N}^{{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{W})=\bm{R}\,\bm{G}_{N}^{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}}\left(\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{\varphi},\overline{\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}}(\bm{W})\right)

where
{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}=({\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\bm{R}
are defined according to
Equation 27
.

Proof.

Let
\bm{W}=(\bm{W}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be any sequence of elements of
\mathcal{M}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}})
and
\bm{\varphi}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
. First, according to
Equation 13
applied to
{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}
, we have:

\bm{G}_{N}^{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{W})=\left(\sum_{n=1}^{N}w\big(\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}(\bm{W}_{n}-\bm{\varphi})\big){\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1}\right)^{\!-1}\!\times\,\sum_{n=1}^{N}w\big(\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}(\bm{W}_{n}-\bm{\varphi})\big){\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1}\bm{W}_{n}\,.

(108)

Second, by injecting
Equation 27b
&
Equation 28b
into
Equation 14
, we obtain

\begin{array}[]{lll}\hskip-4.26773pt\bm{G}_{N}^{{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{W})\\
=\Big(\bm{R}\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{N}w\big(\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}\!\big(\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}(\bm{W}_{n}-\bm{\varphi})\big)\big){\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1}\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\,\Big)^{\!-1}\!\!\times\!\bm{R}\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{N}w\big(\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}\!\big(\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}(\bm{W}_{n}-\bm{\varphi})\big)\big){\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1}\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{W}_{n}\\
=\bm{R}\Big(\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{N}\!w\big(\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}\!\big(\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{W}_{n}\!-\!\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{\varphi}\big)\big){\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1}\Big)^{\!-1}\!\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\!\!\times\!\bm{R}\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{N}\!w\big(\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}\!\big(\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{W}_{n}\!-\!\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{\varphi}\big)\big){\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1}\!\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{W}_{n}\\
=\bm{R}\Big(\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{N}w\big(\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}\!\big(\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{W}_{n}-\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{\varphi}\big)\big){\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1}\Big)^{\!-1}\!\times\,\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{N}w\big(\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}\!\big(\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{W}_{n}-\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{\varphi}\big)\big){\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1}\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{W}_{n}\,,\end{array}

(109)

since
\bm{R}
is orthogonal and, because
w(t)>0
for all
t\in{[0,\infty[}
,

\forall\omega\in\Omega,\sum_{n=1}^{N}w\left(\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}\big(\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{W}_{n}(\omega)-\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{\varphi}\big)\right){\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1}\in\operatorname{GL}_{\mathrm{d}}(\mathbb{R})\,.

Whence the result as a consequence of
Equation 30
,
Equation 108
and the last equality in
Equation 109
.
∎

Suppose that
1
,
2
,
3
and
4
hold for a sequence
\kappa=(\kappa_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
of integers in
\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket
, a function
w:{[0,\infty[}\to{]0,\infty[}
and two sequences of symmetric positive-definite matrices
\bm{C}=(\bm{C}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and
{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}=({{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
. Consider any
\bm{Y}\in{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}})}
, any
\bm{\Phi}\in{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}
and any
\bm{\varphi}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
.

We derive from Lemma
8
that

\bm{G}_{N}^{{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))-\bm{\varphi}=\bm{R}\,\left(\bm{G}_{N}^{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}}(\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{\varphi},\overline{\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}}(\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi})))-\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{\varphi}\right)\,,

(110)

where
{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}=({\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
, the matrices
{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}
and
\bm{R}
being defined by
Equation 27
.

In addition, choose any
\bm{\varphi}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
and any

. Introduce the function
\widetilde{\bm{R}}_{(k_{0},\bm{\varphi}_{0})}:{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\!\bm{\varphi}_{0}}\to{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}
, denoted shortly
\widetilde{\bm{R}}
, assigning to a given

the sequence
\widetilde{\bm{R}}(\bm{\Xi})\in{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}
defined by:

\begin{array}[]{cccc}\widetilde{\bm{R}}(\bm{\Xi}):&\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket&\to&\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}\\
&k&\mapsto&\bm{R}\,\bm{\Xi}(k)\,.\end{array}

(111)

Since
\bm{R}\in\operatorname{GL}_{\mathrm{d}}(\mathbb{R})
,
\widetilde{\bm{R}}
is a bijection and its inverse is
\widetilde{\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}}_{(k_{0},\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{\varphi}_{0})}:{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}\to{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\!\bm{\varphi}_{0}}
— in short,
\widetilde{\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}}
. According to

,

assigns to a given
