\begin{array}[]{cccc}\widetilde{\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}}(\bm{\Phi}):&\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket&\to&\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}\\
&k&\mapsto&\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{\Phi}(k)\,.\end{array}

(112)

According to the definition of
\overline{\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}}
given by
Equation 30
, we construct the composite map

\hskip-5.69046pt\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\overline{\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}}\!\circ\!\bm{Y}\!\circ\!\widetilde{\bm{R}}:&{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\!\bm{\varphi}_{0}}&\!\to&\!{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\Delta}\\
&\bm{\Xi}&\!\mapsto&\!\left(\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}{\bm{Y}\!\left({\widetilde{\bm{R}}(\bm{\Xi}),n}\right)}\right)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\,.\end{array}

(113)

Let
\bm{\Xi}\!\in\!{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\!\bm{\varphi}_{0}}
. We have
\widetilde{\bm{R}}(\bm{\Xi})\!\in\!{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}
and
\bm{Y}(\widetilde{\bm{R}}(\bm{\Xi}))\!\in\!{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}}
. Therefore, since we have
\bm{Y}\!\in\!{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}})}
, it follows that
\operatorname{CtrSeq}_{\kappa,\bm{C}}(\bm{Y}(\widetilde{\bm{R}}(\bm{\Xi})))\!=\!\widetilde{\bm{R}}(\bm{\Xi})
, where we recall that
\operatorname{CtrSeq}_{\kappa,\bm{C}}
is defined by Definition
3
.
Equation 29
thus implies that

\begin{array}[]{lll}\forall n\in\mathbb{N},\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}{\bm{Y}\!\left({\widetilde{\bm{R}}(\bm{\Xi}),n}\right)}&\thicksim&\mathcal{N}\!\left(\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\big(\widetilde{\bm{R}}(\bm{\Xi})\big)(\kappa_{n}),\bm{\Delta}_{n}\right)\\
&\thicksim&\mathcal{N}\!\left(\bm{\Xi}(\kappa_{n}),\bm{\Delta}_{n}\right)\end{array}

(114)

since, by definition of
\widetilde{\bm{R}}
and because
\bm{R}
is orthogonal,
Equation 111
induces that

\forall k\in\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket,\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\big(\widetilde{\bm{R}}(\bm{\Xi})\big)(k)=\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{R}\,\bm{\Xi}(k)=\bm{\Xi}(k)\,.

Thus, for any
\bm{Y}\in{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}})}
and any
\bm{\varphi}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
,

\overline{\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}}\circ\bm{Y}\circ\widetilde{\bm{R}}\in{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\!\bm{\varphi}_{0}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{\Delta}})}\,.

(115)

For any
\bm{\Phi}\in{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}
, it follows from Definition
6
and
Equation 112
that
D^{\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\!\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}\circ\widetilde{\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}}=D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}
. Since
\widetilde{\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}}
is the inverse of
\widetilde{\bm{R}}
, the above equality induces that

D^{\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\!\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}=D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}\circ\widetilde{\bm{R}}\,.

From this equality and
Equation 21
with
H=D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}
and
\gamma^{-1}=\widetilde{\bm{R}}
, we derive that, for any function
f:{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}\to\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})\to\infty}f(\bm{\Phi})=\displaystyle\lim_{D^{\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\!\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Xi})\to\infty}f\big(\widetilde{\bm{R}}(\bm{\Xi})\big)\,.

(116)

Since
\bm{R}
is orthogonal, it follows from
Equation 26
,
Equation 110
and
Equation 116
that

\displaystyle\mathop{\text{ess. lim. sup}}_{D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})\to\infty\,,\,N\to\infty}\bm{G}_{N}^{{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))=\bm{\varphi}

if and only if

\displaystyle\lim_{D^{\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Xi})\to\infty}\left|\underset{\,N\to\infty}{\overline{\lim}}{\Big\|\,\bm{G}_{N}^{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}}(\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{\varphi},\overline{\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}}(\bm{Y}(\widetilde{\bm{R}}(\bm{\Xi})))-\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{\varphi}\Big\|}\,\right|_{\infty}=0\,.

(117)

According to
Equation 115
, for any
\bm{\Xi}\in{\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\!\bm{\varphi}_{0}}
,
\overline{\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}}\circ\bm{Y}\circ\widetilde{\bm{R}}(\bm{\Xi})\in{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{\Delta}}
. Therefore, the covariance matrices of the elements of
\overline{\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}}\circ\bm{Y}\circ\widetilde{\bm{R}}(\bm{\Xi})
are all diagonal. By Equivalence
(\textbf{A})
established in Section
A.2
for diagonal covariance matrices, the equality in
Equation 117
is satisfied if and only if
\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{\varphi}=\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{\varphi}_{0}
, and hence, if and only if
\bm{\varphi}=\bm{\varphi}_{0}
because
\bm{R}\in\operatorname{GL}_{\mathrm{d}}(\mathbb{R})
.

A.4
Proof of Equivalence (
B
)

Suppose that a sequence
\kappa=(\kappa_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
of integers valued in
\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket
, a function
w:{[0,\infty[}\to{]0,\infty[}
and two sequences
\bm{C}=(\bm{C})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and
{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}=({{\bm{C}^{\prime}}})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
of symmetric positive-definite matrices satisfy
1
,
2
,
3
and
4
.

Given any
k_{0}\!\in\!\llbracket 1,K\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket
, any pair
(\bm{\varphi}_{0},\bm{\varphi})\!\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}\!\times\!\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
and any
\bm{Y}\!\in\!{\operatorname{Models}({\mathbb{S}}_{k_{0}}^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}},{\operatorname{Seq}}_{\,\kappa,\bm{C}})}
, it follows from
Equation 25
that

\displaystyle\mathop{\text{ess. lim. sup}}_{D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})\to\infty\,,\,N\to\infty}\bm{G}_{N}^{{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))=\bm{\varphi}\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\displaystyle\mathop{\text{ess. lim. sup}}_{D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})\to\infty\,,\,N\to\infty}\left(\bm{G}_{N}^{{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))-\bm{\varphi}\right)=0\,.

According to
Equation 110
& Proposition
3
, which applies since
\bm{R}\in\operatorname{GL}_{\mathrm{d}}(\mathbb{R})
, we derive from the equivalence above that

\begin{array}[]{lll}\displaystyle\mathop{\text{ess. lim. sup}}_{D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})\to\infty\,,\,N\to\infty}\bm{G}_{N}^{{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))=\bm{\varphi}\\
\hskip 56.9055pt\Leftrightarrow\quad\displaystyle\mathop{\text{ess. lim. sup}}_{D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})\to\infty\,,\,N\to\infty}\left(\bm{G}_{N}^{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}}(\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{\varphi},\overline{\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}}(\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi})))-\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{\varphi}\right)=0\end{array}

and thus, by applying
Equation 25
again, we obtain that

\begin{array}[]{lll}\displaystyle\mathop{\text{ess. lim. sup}}_{D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})\to\infty\,,\,N\to\infty}\bm{G}_{N}^{{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}}(\bm{\varphi},\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi}))=\bm{\varphi}\\
\hskip 56.9055pt\Leftrightarrow\quad\displaystyle\mathop{\text{ess. lim. sup}}_{D^{\bm{\varphi}_{0}}_{k_{0}}(\bm{\Phi})\to\infty\,,\,N\to\infty}\bm{G}_{N}^{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}}(\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{\varphi},\overline{\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}}(\bm{Y}(\bm{\Phi})))=\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{\varphi}\,.\end{array}

Whence the result as a consequence of
(\textbf{A})
.

Appendix B
Instrumental lemmas

In what follows,
\,p_{\mathcal{N}}
denotes the probability density function of
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
and we write (a.e) for “almost everywhere” with respect to the Lebesgue measure on
\mathbb{R}
. As usual,
L^{1}(\mathbb{R})
denotes the set of all integrable functions
f:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
with respect to Lebesgue measure and the
sign
function is defined for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
by setting:

\text{sign}(x)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}1&\text{if}&x>0\,,\\
-1&\text{if}&x<0\,,\\
0&\text{if}&x=0\,.\end{array}\right.

Definition 9
.

We say that a function
\bm{f}:\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}\to{[0,\infty[}
is even in each coordinate of
\bm{x}=(\bm{x}_{1},\ldots,\bm{x}_{m})^{\mathrm{T}}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
if, for any
i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\,\rrbracket
,
\bm{f}(\ldots,-x_{i},\ldots)=\bm{f}(\ldots,x_{i},\ldots)
when the coordinates other than
x_{i}
remain unchanged.

To state and prove Lemmas
9
and
11
below, we introduce the following functions:

\forall(a,b)\in{[0,\infty[}\times{[0,\infty[},\forall x\in{[0,\infty[},u_{a,b}(x)=\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-x^{2}/2}e^{-b^{2}/2}\left(e^{ax}-e^{-ax}\right)\,.

(118)

Lemma 9
.

If
G:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
is odd and non-negative for any
x\geqslant 0
and if, for any
\mu\in\mathbb{R}
,
G\times\,p_{\mathcal{N}}(\cdot-\mu)\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R})
then, for any
\mu\in\mathbb{R}
and any
(a,b)\in{[0,\infty[}\times{[0,\infty[}
such that
0\leqslant a\leqslant|\mu|\leqslant b<\infty
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}G(x)u_{a,b}(x)dx\leqslant\text{sign}(\mu)\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}G(x)\,p_{\mathcal{N}}\left(x-\mu\right)dx\,.

Proof.

Suppose that
G:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
is odd and non-negative for any
x\geqslant 0
. Fix
\mu\in\mathbb{R}
. Readily, we have

\begin{array}[]{lll}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}G(x)\,p_{\mathcal{N}}(x-\mu)dx&=&\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}G(x)\left(e^{-\frac{1}{2}(x-\mu)^{2}}-e^{-\frac{1}{2}(x+\mu)^{2}}\right)dx\Big)dx\\
&=&\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}G(x)u_{\mu,\mu}(x)dx\,.\end{array}

(119)

In addition,

\forall x\in\mathbb{R},\text{sign}(\mu)u_{\mu,\mu}(x)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-x^{2}/2}e^{-\mu^{2}/2}\left(e^{|\mu|x}-e^{-|\mu|x}\right)\,.

(120)

If
(a,b)\in{[0,\infty[}\times{[0,\infty[}
verifies
0\leqslant a\leqslant|\mu|\leqslant b<\infty
, it straightforwardly results from
Equation 120
and
Equation 118
that

\forall x\geqslant 0,\text{sign}(\mu){u_{\mu,\mu}(x)}\geqslant u_{a,b}(x)\geqslant 0\,.

(121)

The result thus derives from
Equation 119
and
Equation 121
, since
G(x)\geqslant 0
if
x\geqslant 0
.
∎

Lemma 10
.

If
F:\mathbb{R}\to{[0,\infty[}
is even and
F\times\,p_{\mathcal{N}}(\cdot-\mu)\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R})
for any
\mu\in\mathbb{R}
then, for any
\mu\in\mathbb{R}
and any pair
(a,b)\in{[0,\infty[}\times{[0,\infty[}
such that
0\leqslant a\leqslant|\mu|\leqslant b<\infty
,

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\hskip-5.69046ptF(x)\,p_{\mathcal{N}}\left(x-\mu\right)dx\geqslant e^{-\frac{1}{2}(b^{2}-a^{2})}\hskip-2.84544pt\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\hskip-5.69046ptF(x)\,p_{\mathcal{N}}\left(x-a\right)dx\,.

Proof.

Since
F
is even, we have:

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}F(x)\,p_{\mathcal{N}}\left(x-\mu\right)dx=\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}F(x)e^{-x^{2}/2}e^{-\mu^{2}/2}\left(e^{\mu x}+e^{-\mu x}\right)dx\,.

(122)

If
(a,b)\in{[0,\infty[}\times{[0,\infty[}
verifies
0\leqslant a\leqslant|\mu|\leqslant b<\infty
, we have the following inequalities:

\forall x\geqslant 0,\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}e^{\mu x}+e^{-\mu x}\geqslant e^{|\mu|x}\geqslant e^{ax}\,,\\
e^{-\mu^{2}/2}\geqslant e^{-b^{2}/2}\,.\end{array}\right.

Thence the result by injecting the inequalities above into
Equation 122
, since
F\geqslant 0
.
∎

Lemma 11
.

Suppose that
[{\lambda}_{\text{min}},{\lambda}_{\text{max}}]\subset{]0,\infty[}
and consider any
\bm{\theta}=(\theta_{1}\ldots,\theta_{\mathrm{d}})^{\mathrm{T}}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
. Let
(\bm{X}_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of
\mathrm{d}
-dimensional real Gaussian random vectors such that

\forall m\in\mathbb{N},\bm{X}_{m}=(X_{m,1},\ldots,X_{m,\mathrm{d}})^{\mathrm{T}}\thicksim\mathcal{N}(\bm{\Lambda}_{m}\bm{\theta},\bm{I}_{\mathrm{d}})

where, for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
\bm{\Lambda}_{m}
is a diagonal matrix whose diagonal elements belong to
[{\lambda}_{\text{min}},{\lambda}_{\text{max}}]
. If
(\bm{f}_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
is a sequence of positive and bounded functions
\bm{f}_{m}:\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}\to{]0,\infty[}
that are even in each coordinate and if there exists a positive function
\bm{f}^{*}:\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}\to{]0,\infty[}
such that
\forall m\in\mathbb{N},\displaystyle\bm{f}^{*}\leqslant\bm{f}_{m}
(a.e), then

\left(\forall i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket,\displaystyle\lim_{M\to\infty}\dfrac{1}{M}\displaystyle\sum_{{m=1}}^{M}\mathbb{E}\big[\bm{f}_{m}(\bm{X}_{m})X_{m,i}\big]=0\right)\,\,\Leftrightarrow\,\,\theta=0\,.

(123)

Proof.

We prove the direct implication only, the converse being straightforwardly true because each
\bm{f}_{m}
is even in its
i^{\text{th}}
variable. Choose arbitrarily any
m\in\mathbb{N}
. To fix ideas, consider the first coordinate
X_{m,1}
of
\bm{X}_{m}
. This choice is convenient to alleviate notation without incurring any loss of generality. For any given
m\in\mathbb{N}
, we put
\bm{\Lambda}_{m}=\operatorname{diag}({\lambda}_{m,1},\ldots,{\lambda}_{m,\mathrm{d}})
. Since
\bm{f}_{m}
is bounded,
\mathbb{E}\big[\,|\,\bm{f}_{m}(\bm{X}_{m})X_{m,1}\,|\,\big]<\infty
. Therefore, we have

\mathbb{E}\big[f_{m}(\bm{X}_{m})X_{m,1}\big]=\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}x_{1}\,g_{m}(x_{1})\,p_{\mathcal{N}}\left(x_{1}-{\lambda}_{m,1}\theta_{1}\right)dx_{1}<\infty

(124)

with

\displaystyle\,\bm{f}_{m}(x_{1})\,,

\displaystyle\text{if $\mathrm{d}=1$}\,,

(125a)

\displaystyle\,\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{{\mathrm{d}}-1}}\bm{f}_{m}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{\mathrm{d}})\prod_{i=2}^{\mathrm{d}}\,p_{\mathcal{N}}\left(x_{i}-{\lambda}_{m,i}\theta_{i}\right)dx_{i}\,,

\displaystyle\text{if $\mathrm{d}\geqslant 2$}\,,

(125b)

where
Equation 125b
is obtained via Fubini’s Theorem. The function
G:x_{1}\in\mathbb{R}\mapsto x_{1}\,g_{m}(x_{1})\in\mathbb{R}
is odd because
\bm{f}_{m}
is even in each of its coordinate. Furthermore,
G(x_{1})\geqslant 0
if
x_{1}\geqslant 0
. We also have
0\leqslant{\lambda}_{\text{min}}|\theta_{1}|\leqslant{\lambda}_{m,1}|\theta_{1}|\leqslant{\lambda}_{\text{max}}|\theta_{1}|<\infty
. In addition,

\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}G(x_{1})\,p_{\mathcal{N}}(x_{1}-{\lambda}_{m,1}\theta_{1})dx<\infty

by
Equation 124
. Thence, Lemma
9
applies to
G
and yields that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}x_{1}g_{m}(x_{1})u(x_{1},\theta_{1})dx_{1}\leqslant\text{sign}({\lambda}_{m,1}\theta_{1})\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}x_{1}\,g_{m}(x_{1})\,p_{\mathcal{N}}(x_{1}-{\lambda}_{m,1}\theta_{1})dx_{1}

(126)

where
u(x_{1},\theta_{1})=u_{{\lambda}_{\text{min}}|\theta_{1}|,{\lambda}_{\text{max}}|\theta_{1}|}(x_{1})
is calculated according to
Equation 118
. Since
{\lambda}_{m,1}>0
,
\text{sign}({\lambda}_{m,1}\theta_{1})=\text{sign}(\theta_{1})
. We thus derive from
Equation 126
and
Equation 124
that

\hskip-14.22636pt\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\hskip-7.11317ptx_{1}g_{m}(x_{1})u(x_{1},\theta_{1})dx_{1}\leqslant\text{sign}(\theta_{1})\,\mathbb{E}\big[{f_{m}}(\bm{X}_{m})X_{m,1}\big]\hskip-11.38092pt

(127)

Set
c^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\left({\lambda}_{\text{max}}^{2}-{\lambda}_{\text{min}}^{2}\right)
and suppose that
\mathrm{d}\geqslant 2
.
We now prove that, for any
j\in{\llbracket 2,\mathrm{d}\,\rrbracket}
, any
x_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
and, if
j\leqslant\mathrm{d}-1
, any real values
x_{j+1},\ldots,x_{\mathrm{d}}
,

\hskip-9.95863pt\begin{array}[]{lll}\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{j-1}}\bm{f}_{m}(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{\mathrm{d}})\prod_{i=2}^{j}\,p_{\mathcal{N}}(x_{i}-{\lambda}_{m,i}\theta_{i})dx_{i}\\
\hskip 56.9055pt\geqslant\,e^{-c^{2}\sum_{i=2}^{j}\theta_{i}^{2}}\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{j-1}}\bm{f}_{m}(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{\mathrm{d}})\prod_{i=2}^{j}\,p_{\mathcal{N}}(x_{i}-{\lambda}_{\text{min}}|\theta_{i}|)dx_{i}\,.\end{array}

(128)

We proceed by recurrence and begin with
j=2
. Consider any
x_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
and, if
\mathrm{d}\geqslant 3
, any
x_{3},\ldots,x_{\mathrm{d}}
in
\mathbb{R}
. We then define
F_{2}:\mathbb{R}\to{[0,\infty[}
for every
x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
by setting

F_{2}(x_{2})=\bm{f}_{m}(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{\mathrm{d}})\,.

The function
F_{2}
is even and

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}F_{2}(x_{2})\,p_{\mathcal{N}}(x_{2}-{\lambda}_{m,2}\theta_{2})dx_{2}<\infty

because
\bm{f}_{m}
is bounded. Since
0\leqslant{\lambda}_{\text{min}}|\theta_{2}|\leqslant{\lambda}_{m,2}|\theta_{2}|\leqslant{\lambda}_{\text{max}}|\theta_{2}|<\infty
, Lemma
10
applied to
F_{2}
induces that

\begin{array}[]{lll}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\bm{f}_{m}(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{\mathrm{d}})\,p_{\mathcal{N}}(x_{2}-{\lambda}_{m,2}\theta_{2})dx_{2}\\
\hskip 56.9055pt\geqslant\,e^{-c^{2}\theta_{2}^{2}}\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\bm{f}_{m}(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{\mathrm{d}})\,p_{\mathcal{N}}(x_{2}-{\lambda}_{\text{min}}|\theta_{2}|)dx_{2}\end{array}

and
Equation 128
is thus verified for
j=2
. If
\mathrm{d}=2
, the recurrence stops here.

Suppose that
\mathrm{d}\geqslant 3
and that
Equation 128
is satisfied for a given
j\in{\llbracket 2,\mathrm{d}-1\,\rrbracket}
. Given any
x_{1}\!\in\!\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
and any given real values
x_{j+1},\ldots,x_{\mathrm{d}}
, the Fubini theorem implies:

\begin{array}[]{lll}\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{j}}\bm{f}_{m}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{\mathrm{d}})\prod_{i=2}^{j+1}\,p_{\mathcal{N}}(x_{i}-{\lambda}_{m,i}\theta_{i})dx_{i}\\
=\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{j-1}}\!\bm{f}_{m}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{\mathrm{d}})\prod_{i=2}^{j}\,p_{\mathcal{N}}(x_{i}-{\lambda}_{m,i}\theta_{i})dx_{i}\bigg)\times\,p_{\mathcal{N}}(x_{j+1}-{\lambda}_{m,j+1}\theta_{j+1})dx_{j+1}\,.\end{array}

Therefore, according to
Equation 128
, we obtain:

\begin{array}[]{lll}\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{j}}\bm{f}_{m}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{\mathrm{d}})\prod_{i=2}^{j+1}\,p_{\mathcal{N}}(x_{i}-{\lambda}_{m,i}\theta_{i})dx_{i}\\
\hskip 28.45274pt\geqslant e^{-c^{2}\sum_{i=2}^{j}\theta_{i}^{2}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\hskip-2.84544pt\bigg(\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{j-1}}\bm{f}_{m}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{\mathrm{d}})\prod_{i=2}^{j}\,p_{\mathcal{N}}\left(x_{i}-{\lambda}_{\text{min}}|\theta_{i}|\right)dx_{i}\bigg)\\
\hskip 227.62204pt\times\,\,p_{\mathcal{N}}(x_{j+1}-{\lambda}_{m,j+1}\theta_{j+1})dx_{j+1}\,,\end{array}

(129)

Define the map
F_{j+1}:\mathbb{R}\to{[0,\infty[}
by setting:

\forall x_{j+1}\in\mathbb{R},F_{j+1}(x_{j+1})=e^{-c^{2}\sum_{i=2}^{j}\theta_{i}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{j-1}}\bm{f}_{m}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{\mathrm{d}})\prod_{i=2}^{j}\,p_{\mathcal{N}}(x_{i}-{\lambda}_{\text{min}}|\theta_{i}|)dx_{i}\,.

This function is even and
\bm{f}_{m}
being bounded,
Equation 129
implies that
F_{j+1}\,p_{\mathcal{N}}(\cdot-{\lambda}_{m,j+1}\theta_{j+1})\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R})
. As
0\leqslant{\lambda}_{\text{min}}|\theta_{j+1}|\leqslant{\lambda}_{m,j+1}|\theta_{j+1}|\leqslant{\lambda}_{\text{max}}|\theta_{j+1}|<\infty
, we apply Lemma
10
to
F_{j+1}
, which leads to:

\hskip-2.84544pt\begin{array}[]{lll}e^{-c^{2}\sum_{i=2}^{j}\theta_{i}^{2}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\hskip-2.84544pt\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{j-1}}\hskip-2.84544pt\bm{f}_{m}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{\mathrm{d}})\prod_{i=2}^{j}\!\,p_{\mathcal{N}}(x_{i}\!-\!{\lambda}_{\text{min}}|\theta_{i}|)dx_{i}\!\bigg)\\
\hskip 199.16928pt\times\,\,p_{\mathcal{N}}(x_{j+1}-{\lambda}_{m,j+1}\theta_{j+1})dx_{j+1}\\
\hskip 28.45274pt\geqslant\,e^{-c^{2}\sum_{i=2}^{j+1}\theta_{i}^{2}}\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{j}}\hskip-2.84544pt\bm{f}_{m}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{\mathrm{d}})\prod_{i=2}^{j+1}\,p_{\mathcal{N}}(x_{i}-{\lambda}_{\text{min}}|\theta_{i}|)dx_{i}\,.\end{array}\vskip 2.84544pt

(130)

The recurrence follows by combining
Equation 129
and
Equation 130
.

When
j=\mathrm{d}
and
\mathrm{d}\geqslant 2
,
Equation 128
,
Equation 125b
and the inequality
\sum_{i=2}^{j}\theta_{i}^{2}\leqslant\|\bm{\theta}\|^{2}
imply that:

\forall x_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}},g_{m}(x_{1})\geqslant e^{-c^{2}\|\bm{\theta}\|^{2}}\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{{\mathrm{d}}-1}}\bm{f}_{m}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{\mathrm{d}})\prod_{i=2}^{{\mathrm{d}}}\,p_{\mathcal{N}}(x_{i}-{\lambda}_{\text{min}}|\theta_{i}|)dx_{i}\,.

(131)

Since
\bm{f}_{m}(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{\mathrm{d}})\geqslant\bm{f}^{*}(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{\mathrm{d}})>0
for almost every
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{\mathrm{d}})\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
,
Equation 131
and
Equation 125
induce the following lower bound:

\forall x_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}},g_{m}(x_{1})\geqslant\Upsilon(x_{1})

(132)

with

\Upsilon(x_{1})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\hskip-2.84544pt\bm{f}^{*}(x_{1})\,,&\text{if $\mathrm{d}=1$}\,,\\
\hskip-2.84544pte^{-c^{2}\|\bm{\theta}\|^{2}}\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\mathrm{d}-1}}\bm{f}^{*}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{\mathrm{d})}\,\prod_{i=2}^{{\mathrm{d}}}\,p_{\mathcal{N}}(x_{i}-{\lambda}_{\text{min}}|\theta_{i}|)dx_{i}\,,&\text{if $\mathrm{d}\geqslant 2$}\,.\end{array}\right.

(133)

We now derive from
Equation 127
,
Equation 132
and
Equation 133
that

\forall m\in\mathbb{N},0\leqslant\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}x_{1}\Upsilon(x_{1})u(x_{1},\theta_{1})dx_{1}\leqslant\text{sign}(\theta_{1})\mathbb{E}\big[f_{m}(\bm{X}_{m})X_{m,1}\big]

and thus that

0\leqslant\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}x_{1}\Upsilon(x_{1})u(x_{1},\theta_{1})dx_{1}\leqslant\text{sign}(\theta_{1})\frac{1}{M}\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{M}\mathbb{E}\big[f_{m}(\bm{X}_{m})X_{m,1}\big]\,.

Suppose that
\lim\limits_{M\to\infty}\frac{1}{M}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\mathbb{E}\big[f_{m}(\bm{X}_{m})X_{m,1}\big]=0
. The inequalities above then imply that

\int_{0}^{\infty}x_{1}\Upsilon(x_{1})u(x_{1},\theta_{1})dx_{1}=0

and thus that
x_{1}\Upsilon(x_{1})u(x_{1},\theta_{1})=0
for almost every
x_{1}\in{[0,\infty[}
. Because
x_{1}\Upsilon(x_{1})>0
for any
x_{1}>0
,
x_{1}\Upsilon(x_{1})u(x_{1},\theta_{1})=0
for almost every
x_{1}\in{[0,\infty[}
implies that
u(x_{1},\theta_{1})=0
for almost every
x_{1}\in{]0,\infty[}
and thus, that
\theta_{1}=0
because, as shown by a straightforward computation, for any given
x_{1}\neq 0
,
u(x_{1},\theta_{1})=0
if and only if
\theta_{1}=0
. We can reiterate the same reasoning for any other
i=2,\ldots,\mathrm{d}
to conclude.
∎

In
Equation 19
, take the Euclidean norm
\|\cdot\|
in
\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
for
H
. Given a function
f:\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}\to\mathbb{R}
, we derive from
Equation 20
that

\displaystyle\lim_{\|\bm{x}\|\to\infty}f(\bm{x})=0\Leftrightarrow\big(\,\forall\eta\in{]0,\infty[},\exists\,r\in{[0,\infty[},\forall\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}},\|\bm{x}\|>r\Rightarrow|f(\bm{x})|<\eta\,\big)\,.

(134)

Similarly,
{L^{1}}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}})
being the set of all
\bm{X}\in\mathcal{M}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}})
such that
\mathbb{E}\big[\|\bm{X}\|\big]<\infty
, consider

\begin{array}[]{ccccc}H:&{L^{1}}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}})&\to&{[0,\infty[}\\
&\bm{X}&\mapsto&\|\,\mathbb{E}\,[\bm{X}]\,\|\end{array}

in
Equation 19
. Given
f:{L^{1}}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}})\to{[0,\infty[}
, it follows from
Equation 20
that

\begin{array}[]{lll}\hskip-11.38092pt\displaystyle\lim_{\|\,\mathbb{E}\,[\bm{X}]\,\|\to\infty}\bm{f}(\bm{X})=0\\
\Leftrightarrow\big(\forall\eta\in{]0,\infty[},\exists\,r\in{[0,\infty[},\forall\bm{X}\in\mathcal{M}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}),\|\,\mathbb{E}\,[\bm{X}]\,\|>r\Rightarrow|f(\bm{x})|<\eta\big)\,.\end{array}

(135)

Lemma 12
.

Let
\bm{X}(\bm{\xi})\thicksim\mathcal{N}(\bm{\xi},\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{{\mathrm{d}}})
with
\sigma>0
and
g:{[0,\infty[}\to{[0,\infty[}
be a non-null continuous function. If
\displaystyle\lim\limits_{t\rightarrow\infty}{g(t)}=0
then

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\|\bm{\xi}\|\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[g(\|\bm{X}(\bm{\xi})\|)\big]=0\,.

Proof.

This straightforward application of the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem is provided for self-completeness of the paper.

Let
g:{[0,\infty[}\to{[0,\infty[}
be a non-null continuous function and suppose that
\bm{X}(\bm{\xi})\thicksim\mathcal{N}(\bm{\xi},\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{{\mathrm{d}}})
with
\sigma>0
. We have:

\begin{array}[]{lll}\mathbb{E}\big[g(\|\bm{X}(\bm{\xi})\|)\big]&=&\dfrac{1}{(2\pi)^{{\mathrm{d}}/2}\sigma^{\mathrm{d}}}\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}}g\big(\|\bm{x}\|\big)e^{-\|\bm{x}-\bm{\xi}\|/2\sigma^{2}}d\bm{x}\\
&=&\dfrac{1}{(2\pi)^{{\mathrm{d}}/2}\sigma^{\mathrm{d}}}\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}}g\big(\|\bm{y}+\bm{\xi}\|\big)e^{-\|\bm{y}\|/2\sigma^{2}}d\bm{y}\,.\end{array}

(136)

Since
g
is continuous and
\displaystyle\lim\limits_{t\rightarrow\infty}{g(t)}=0
,
g
is bounded and so are the values
g\left(\|\bm{y}+\bm{\xi}\|\right)
for
(\bm{y},\bm{\xi})\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}\times\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
. In addition, given any
\bm{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
, since
\displaystyle\lim\limits_{t\rightarrow\infty}{g(t)}=0
, it results from
Equation 134
that
\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\|\bm{\xi}\|\rightarrow\infty}g\left(\|\bm{y}+\bm{\xi}\|\right)=0
. The result thus follows from
Equation 136
and the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem.
∎

We now use Definition
8
introduced in Appendix
A
to state our next result.

Lemma 13
.

If
[{\lambda}_{\text{min}},{\lambda}_{\text{max}}]\!\subset\!{]0,\infty[}
and
g:\!{[0,\infty[}\!\to\!{[0,\infty[}
is a continuous function such that
\displaystyle\lim\limits_{t\rightarrow\infty}g(t)=0
, then

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\|\mathbb{E}[{\bm{X}}]\|\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[g(\|\bm{X}\|)\big]=0

uniformly in
\bm{X}\in{\operatorname{GDiag}}{[{{\lambda}_{\text{min}}^{2}},{{\lambda}_{\text{max}}^{2}}]}
in that:

\begin{array}[]{c}\forall\,\varepsilon>0,\exists\,r>0,\forall\bm{X}\in{\operatorname{GDiag}}{[{{\lambda}_{\text{min}}^{2}},{{\lambda}_{\text{max}}^{2}}]},\|\,\mathbb{E}[{\bm{X}}]\,\|>r\Rightarrow\,\mathbb{E}\big[\,g(\,\|\bm{X}\|\,)\,\big]<\varepsilon\,.\end{array}

(137)

Proof.

Given any
\bm{\xi}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
, set

F(\bm{\xi})=\dfrac{1}{(2\pi)^{\mathrm{d}/2}{\lambda}_{\text{min}}^{\mathrm{d}}}\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}}g\big(\|\bm{x}\|\big)e^{-\|\bm{x}-\bm{\xi}\|^{2}/2{\lambda}_{\text{max}}^{2}}d\bm{x}=\dfrac{{\lambda}_{\text{max}}^{\mathrm{d}}}{{\lambda}_{\text{min}}^{\mathrm{d}}}\,\mathbb{E}\big[g(\|\bm{Z}(\bm{\xi})\|)\big]

where
\bm{Z}(\bm{\xi})
is any
\mathrm{d}
-dimensional real random vector with distribution
\mathcal{N}(\bm{\xi},{\lambda}_{\text{max}}^{2}\,\bm{I}_{{\mathrm{d}}})
. It follows from Lemma
12
that
\lim\limits_{\|\bm{\xi}\|\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[g(\|\bm{Z}(\bm{\xi})\|)\big]=0
and thus that

\lim\limits_{\|\bm{\xi}\|\to\infty}F(\bm{\xi})=0\,.

(138)

Consider any
\bm{X}\in{\operatorname{GDiag}}{[{{\lambda}_{\text{min}}^{2}},{\lambda}_{\text{max}}^{2}]}
. Let
{\lambda}^{2}_{1},\ldots,{\lambda}^{2}_{\mathrm{d}}
be the diagonal elements of the covariance matrix of
\bm{X}
and set
\bm{\xi}=(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{\mathrm{d}})^{\mathrm{T}}=\mathbb{E}[\bm{X}]
. We thus have:

\mathbb{E}\big[g(\|\bm{X}\|)\big]=\dfrac{1}{(2\pi)^{\mathrm{d}/2}\,\prod_{i=1}^{\mathrm{d}}\!{\lambda}_{i}}\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}}g\big(\|\bm{x}\|\big)e^{-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{\mathrm{d}}\!{(\bm{x}_{i}-\xi_{i})^{2}}/{{\lambda}^{2}_{i}}}\,d\bm{x}\,.

Since
\prod_{i=1}^{\mathrm{d}}\!{\lambda}_{i}\geqslant{\lambda}_{\text{min}}^{\mathrm{d}}
and
\sum_{i=1}^{\mathrm{d}}\!{(x_{i}-\bm{\xi}_{i})^{2}}/{{\lambda}^{2}_{i}}\!\geqslant\!\|\bm{x}-\bm{\xi}\|^{2}/{{\lambda}_{\text{max}}^{2}}
for any
\bm{x}\!\in\!\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
, we have

\mathbb{E}\big[g(\|\bm{X}\|)\big]\leqslant F(\bm{\xi})
. Thence the result as a consequence of
Equation 138
.
∎

Appendix C
Proof of Proposition
1

This proof, up to
Equation 145
below, relies on the same type of arguments as in the Appendix of
9
. To begin with, through the same steps as those employed to derive
Equation 109
, it follows from
Equation 8
that:

\hskip-8.5359pt\begin{array}[]{lll}\forall\,(\ell,N)\in\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N},\\
\hskip 14.22636pt\begin{array}[]{lll}\bm{\varphi}^{(\ell+1)}&=&\bm{g}_{N}(\bm{\varphi}^{(\ell)})\\
&=&\bm{R}\left(\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{N}w\big(\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}(\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{y}_{n}-\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{\varphi}^{(\ell)})\big){\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1}\right)^{\!-1}\\
&&\hskip 56.9055pt\times\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{N}w\big(\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}(\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{y}_{n}-\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{\varphi}^{(\ell)})\big){\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1}\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{y}_{n}\,.\end{array}\end{array}

(139)

For any
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, set
\bm{\xi}^{(\ell)}=\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{\varphi}^{(\ell)}
and, in addition, for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\bm{z}_{n}=\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{y}_{n}
. We can thus rewrite
Equation 139
as:

\hskip-8.5359pt\begin{array}[]{lll}\forall\,{(\ell,N)\in\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}},\\
\hskip 8.5359pt\begin{array}[]{lll}\bm{\xi}^{(\ell+1)}&=&\bm{R}^{\mathrm{T}}\bm{\varphi}^{(\ell+1)}\\
&=&\left(\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{N}w\big(\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}(\bm{z}_{n}-\bm{\xi}^{(\ell)})\big){\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1}\right)^{\!-1}\hskip-2.84544pt\times\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{N}w\big(\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}(\bm{z}_{n}-\bm{\xi}^{(\ell)})\big){\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1}\bm{z}_{n}\,.\end{array}\end{array}

(140)

For any
(\ell,N)\in\mathbb{N}\times{\mathbb{N}}
, set

\varrho_{N}(\ell)=\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathcal{R}(\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}(\bm{\xi}^{(\ell)}-\bm{z}_{n}))\,,

where
\mathcal{R}
has derivative
w
according to
Equation 6
.
Since
w
is non-increasing and differentiable, we have
\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}=w^{\prime}\leqslant 0
. It follows that
\mathcal{R}
increases — because
w
is valued in
{]0,\infty[}
— and concave. The concavity of
\mathcal{R}
,
Equation 6
and
Equation 1
imply that, for any
(\ell,n)\in\mathbb{N}\times{\llbracket 1,N\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket}
,

\begin{array}[]{lll}\mathcal{R}(\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}(\bm{\xi}^{(\ell+1)}-\bm{z}_{n}))-\mathcal{R}(\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}(\bm{\xi}^{(\ell)}-\bm{z}_{n}))\\
\qquad\leqslant w\left(\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}(\bm{\xi}^{(\ell)}\!-\!\bm{z}_{n})\right)\times\big(\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}(\bm{\xi}^{(\ell+1)}-\bm{z}_{n})-\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}(\bm{\xi}^{(\ell)}-\bm{z}_{n})\big)\\
\qquad\leqslant 2w\left(\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}(\bm{\xi}^{(\ell)}\!-\!\bm{z}_{n})\right)\big(\bm{\theta}^{(\ell)}\!-\!\bm{z}_{n}\big)^{\mathrm{\!T}}{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1}\bm{D}^{(\ell)}\end{array}

with

\displaystyle\bm{\theta}^{(\ell)}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\bm{\xi}^{(\ell+1)}+\bm{\xi}^{(\ell)}\right)\,,

(141a)

\displaystyle\bm{D}^{(\ell)}=\bm{\xi}^{(\ell+1)}-\bm{\xi}^{(\ell)}\,,

(141b)

since

\displaystyle\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}(\bm{\xi}^{(\ell+1)}

\displaystyle-\bm{z}_{n})-\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}(\bm{\xi}^{(\ell)}-\bm{z}_{n})\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0pt

\displaystyle=\Big(\bm{\xi}^{(\ell+1)}-\bm{z}_{n}+\bm{\xi}^{(\ell)}-\bm{z}_{n}\Big)^{\mathrm{T}}{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1}\Big(\bm{\xi}^{(\ell+1)}-\bm{z}_{n}-\big(\bm{\xi}^{(\ell}-\bm{z}_{n}\big)\Big)\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0pt

\displaystyle=2\left(\bm{\theta}^{(\ell)}-\bm{z}_{n}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1}\bm{D}^{(\ell)}\,.

We derive from the foregoing that, for any
(\ell,N)\in\mathbb{N}\times{\mathbb{N}}
,

\varrho_{N}(\ell+1)-\varrho_{N}(\ell)\leqslant 2\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{N}w\big(\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}(\bm{\xi}^{(\ell)}-\bm{z}_{n})\big)\big(\bm{\theta}^{(\ell)}-\bm{z}_{n}\big)^{\mathrm{\!T}}{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1}\bm{D}^{(\ell)}\,.

(142)

According to
Equation 140
, given
(\ell,N)\in\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}
, we have:

\sum_{n=1}^{N}w\big(\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}(\bm{\xi}^{(\ell)}-\bm{z}_{n})\big){\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1}\bm{z}_{n}=\sum_{n=1}^{N}w\big(\,\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}(\bm{\xi}^{(\ell)}-\bm{z}_{n})\big){\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1}\bm{\xi}^{(\ell+1)}\,.

(143)

For any given
(\ell,N)\in\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}
, we derive from
Equation 141
and
Equation 143
that

\sum_{n=1}^{N}w\big(\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}(\bm{\xi}^{(\ell)}-\bm{z}_{n})\big){\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1}\big(\bm{\theta}^{(\ell)}-\bm{z}_{n}\big)=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{n=1}^{N}w\big(\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}(\bm{\xi}^{(\ell)}-\bm{z}_{n})\big){\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1}\bm{D}^{(\ell)}

and thus, according to
Equation 1
for the definition of the Mahalanobis norm, that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{N}w

\displaystyle\big(\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}(\bm{\xi}^{(\ell)}-\bm{z}_{n})\big)\big(\bm{\theta}^{(\ell)}-\bm{z}_{n}\big)^{\mathrm{T}}{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1}\bm{D}^{(\ell)}\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0pt

\displaystyle=\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{N}w\big(\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}(\bm{\xi}^{(\ell)}-\bm{z}_{n})\big)(\bm{D}^{(\ell)})^{\mathrm{T}}{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}^{\,-1}\big(\bm{\theta}^{(\ell)}-\bm{z}_{n}\big)\vskip 6.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 2.0pt

\displaystyle=-\dfrac{1}{2}\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{N}w\big(\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}(\bm{\xi}^{(\ell)}-\bm{z}_{n})\big)\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}(\bm{D}^{(\ell)})

By injecting the last equality into above into
Equation 142
, we obtain:

\forall(\ell,N)\in\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N},\varrho_{N}(\ell+1)-\varrho_{N}(\ell)\leqslant-\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{N}w\big(\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}(\bm{\xi}^{(\ell)}-\bm{z}_{n})\big)\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}(\bm{D}^{(\ell)})\leqslant 0\,.

(144)

Therefore, the sequence
\left(\varrho_{N}(\ell)\right)_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}
does not increase. Since
\varrho_{N}(\ell)\geqslant 0
for any
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, this sequence has a limit.

Given any
(\ell,L,N)\in\mathbb{N}\times{\mathbb{N}}\times{\mathbb{N}}
,
Equation 144
induces now that

|\varrho_{N}(\ell+L)-\varrho_{N}(\ell)|\geqslant\sum_{m=1}^{L}\left(\,\sum_{n=1}^{N}w\big(\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}(\bm{\xi}^{(\ell+m-1)}-\bm{z}_{n})\big)\right)\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}(\bm{D}^{(\ell+m-1)})\,.

(145)

We now set

\forall n\in\mathbb{N},{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}=\operatorname{diag}(\,{\sigma^{\prime}}^{\,2}_{\!\!\!n,1},\ldots,{\sigma^{\prime}}^{\,2}_{\!\!\!n,\mathrm{d}}\,)\,,

(146)

where, for any given
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the positive real values
{\sigma^{\prime}}^{\,2}_{\!\!\!n,1},\ldots,{\sigma^{\prime}}^{\,2}_{\!\!\!n,\mathrm{d}}
are the eigenvalues of
{{\bm{C}^{\prime}}}_{\!\!n}
and thus verify:

\forall n\in\mathbb{N},\forall i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket,{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!n,i}\in[{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!\text{min}},{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!\text{max}}]\,.

(147)

Hence, we have:

\forall n\in\mathbb{N},\forall\bm{x}=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{\mathrm{d}})^{\mathrm{T}}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}},\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}(\bm{x})=\sqrt{\textstyle\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{\mathrm{d}}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!n,i}^{\,-2}\,x^{2}_{i}}\,,

(148)

from which we derive that

\forall{(\ell,n)\in\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}},\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}(\bm{\xi}^{(\ell)}-\bm{z}_{n})\leqslant{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!\text{min}}^{\,-1}\times\|\bm{\xi}^{(\ell)}-\bm{z}_{n}\|\,.

(149)

For any
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, set
\bm{\xi}^{(\ell)}=\big(\xi^{(\ell)}_{1},\ldots,\xi^{(\ell)}_{\mathrm{d}}\big)^{\mathrm{T}}
and, for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\bm{z}_{n}=(z_{n,1},\ldots,z_{n,\mathrm{d}})^{\mathrm{T}}
. We obtain from
Equation 140
and
Equation 148
that:

\forall(\ell,N)\in\mathbb{N}\times{\mathbb{N}},\forall i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket,\xi_{i}^{(\ell+1)}=\dfrac{\textstyle\sum_{n=1}^{N}w\left(\textstyle\sum_{j=1}^{\mathrm{d}}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!n,j}^{-2}\,\big(\bm{z}_{n}-\bm{\xi}^{(\ell)}\big)_{\!j}^{\!2}\right){\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!n,i}^{\,-2}\,z_{n,i}}{\textstyle\sum_{n=1}^{N}w\left(\textstyle\sum_{j=1}^{\mathrm{d}}{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!n,j}^{-2}\,\big(\bm{z}_{n}-\bm{\xi}^{(\ell)}\big)_{\!j}^{\!2}\right){\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!n,i}^{\,-2}}\,.

(150)

Therefore, for any
\ell\in\llbracket 2,\infty\llbracket
, any
i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket
,
\xi_{i}^{(\ell)}
is a barycenter of the
N
real values
z_{1,i},\ldots,z_{N,i}
. Given any vector space
E
endowed with a norm
\nu
, if
x^{*}=\textstyle\sum_{n=1}^{N}\alpha_{n}x_{n}
is the barycenter of
N
vectors
x_{1},\ldots,x_{N}
of
E
respectively assigned to
N
positive real coefficients
\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{N}
such that
\sum_{n=1}^{N}\alpha_{n}=1
then, for any
j\in\llbracket 1,N\rrbracket
,

\nu(x^{*}-x_{j})=\nu\Big(\sum_{r=1}^{N}\alpha_{r}(x_{r}-x_{j})\Big)\leqslant\sum_{r=1}^{N}\alpha_{r}\nu(x_{r}-x_{j}),

from which we derive that:

\nu(x^{*}-x_{j})\leqslant\text{max}\big\{\nu(x_{r}-x_{s}):(r,s)\in\llbracket 1,N\rrbracket\times\llbracket 1,N\rrbracket\big\}\,.

(151)

Hence, from
Equation 150
&
Appendix C
with
E=\mathbb{R}
and
\nu=|\cdot|
, we obtain that, for any
N\in{\mathbb{N}}
, any
n\in\llbracket 1,N\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket
, any
i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket
and any
\ell\in\llbracket 2,\infty\llbracket
:

|\xi_{i}^{(\ell)}-z_{n,i}|\leqslant\max_{(r,s)\in\llbracket 1,N\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket\times\llbracket 1,N\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket}\{|\,z_{r,i}-z_{s,i}\,|\}\,.

As a consequence, given
\ell\in\llbracket 2,\infty\llbracket
,
n\in\llbracket 1,N\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket
and
i\in\llbracket 1,\mathrm{d}\hskip 0.28436pt\rrbracket
,

\|\xi^{(\ell)}-\bm{z}_{n}\|^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathrm{d}}(\xi_{i}^{(\ell)}-z_{n,i})^{2}\leqslant\mathrm{d}\times\mathrm{M}^{2}

(152)

where
\mathrm{M}=\max_{1\leqslant i\leqslant\mathrm{d}}\left(\max_{(r,s)\in\llbracket 1,N\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket\times\llbracket 1,N\hskip 0.42677pt\rrbracket}\{|\,z_{r,i}-z_{s,i}\,|\}\right)
.
Since
w
does not increase,
Equation 149
&
Equation 152
imply that

\forall{(\ell,n)\in\llbracket 2,\infty\llbracket\times\mathbb{N}},w(\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}\!(\bm{\xi}^{(\ell)}-\bm{z}_{n}))\geqslant w({{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!\text{min}}^{-2}}\times\mathrm{d}\times\mathrm{M}^{2})\,.

Thence the following lower bound

\forall(\ell,N,m)\in\llbracket 2,\infty\llbracket\times{\mathbb{N}}\times\mathbb{N},\sum_{n=1}^{N}w(\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}(\bm{\xi}^{(\ell+m)}-\bm{z}_{n}))\geqslant A

with
A=N\times w({{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!\text{min}}^{-2}}\times\mathrm{d}\times\mathrm{M}^{2})
. Injecting the inequality above into
Equation 145
gives:

\forall(\ell,N,L)\in\llbracket 3,\infty\llbracket\times{\mathbb{N}}\times{\mathbb{N}},|\varrho_{N}(\ell+L)-\varrho_{N}(\ell)|\geqslant A\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{L}\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}\!(\bm{D}^{(\ell+m-1)})\,.

(153)

Fix
\varepsilon\in{]0,\infty[}
. Since the sequence
\left(\varrho_{N}(\ell)\right)_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}
has a limit, it is Cauchy.
Therefore, there exists
\ell_{0}(\varepsilon)\in\llbracket 3,\infty\llbracket
such that

\forall{(\ell,L)\in\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}},\ell\geqslant\ell_{0}(\varepsilon)\Rightarrow|\varrho_{N}(\ell+L)-\varrho_{N}(\ell)|\leqslant A\varepsilon^{2}/\sigma^{\,2}_{\text{max}}\,.

(154)

According to
Equation 146
and
Equation 148
, we have

\forall\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}},\forall n\in\mathbb{N},\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}(\bm{x})=\|{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}^{-1/2}\bm{x}\,\|\geqslant{{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\!\!\text{max}}^{\,-1}}\|\bm{x}\|\,.

(155)

Thereby, it results from
Equation 155
,
Equation 153
and
Equation 154
that, for any integer
\ell\geqslant\ell_{0}(\varepsilon)
and any
L\in{\mathbb{N}}
,

\sum_{m=1}^{L}\|\bm{D}^{(\ell+m-1)}\|^{2}\leqslant\sigma^{\,2}_{\text{max}}\sum_{m=1}^{L}\nu_{{\bm{\Delta}^{\prime}}_{\!\!n}}^{2}\big(\bm{D}^{(\ell+m-1)}\big)\leqslant\sigma^{\,2}_{\text{max}}\,|\varrho_{N}(\ell+L)-\varrho_{N}(\ell)|\,/\,A\leqslant\varepsilon^{2}\,.

(156)

According to
Equation 141b
,

\forall\ell\in\mathbb{N},\bm{\xi}^{(\ell+L)}-\bm{\xi}^{(\ell)}=\sum_{m=1}^{L}\bm{D}^{(\ell+m-1)}\,.

(157)

Straightforwardly, we also have:

\forall L\in\mathbb{N},\forall r\in\llbracket 1,L\rrbracket,\|\bm{D}^{(\ell+r-1)}\|\leqslant\sqrt{\sum_{m=1}^{L}\|\bm{D}^{(\ell+m-1)}\|^{2}}\,.

(158)

As a consequence, for any
L\in{\mathbb{N}}
and any
\ell\geqslant\ell_{0}(\varepsilon)
,

\begin{array}[]{llllll}\|\bm{\varphi}^{(\ell+L)}-\bm{\varphi}^{(\ell)}\|^{2}&=&\hskip-5.69046pt\|\bm{\xi}^{(\ell+L)}-\bm{\xi}^{(\ell)}\|^{2}&\text{[$\bm{R}$ is orthogonal]}\\
&\leqslant&\|\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{L}\bm{D}^{(\ell+m-1)}\|^{2}&\text{[By \autoref{eq: equality a}]}\\
&\hskip-5.69046pt\leqslant&\hskip-5.69046pt\Big(\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{L}\|\bm{D}^{(\ell+m-1)}\|\Big)^{2}\\
&\hskip-5.69046pt\leqslant&\hskip-5.69046ptL^{2}\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{L}\|\bm{D}^{(\ell+m-1)}\|^{2}&\text{[By \autoref{eq: equality b}]}\\
&\hskip-5.69046pt\leqslant&\hskip-5.69046ptL^{2}\varepsilon^{2}&[\text{By \autoref{eq: final inequality for the stoppping criterion}}]\,.\end{array}

Appendix D
p-value of Wald’s test for Gaussian mean testing

Suppose that
\bm{X}\thicksim\mathcal{N}(\bm{\xi},\bm{S})
, with
\bm{\xi}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
and
\bm{S}
is a
\mathrm{d}\times\mathrm{d}
symmetric positive-definite matrix. The critical region of the Wald test
\mathfrak{T}_{\bm{S}}^{\alpha}
given by
Equation 36
is defined as

\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}=\big\{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}:\mathfrak{T}_{\bm{S}}^{\alpha}(\bm{x})=1\big\}=\big\{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}:\mu(\alpha)<\nu_{\bm{S}}(\bm{x})\big\}\,,

(159)

where
\mu(\alpha)
is defined by
Equation 35
. It straightforwardly follows from
Equation 35
and the properties of
\mathbb{F}_{\chi^{2}_{\mathrm{d}}}
that the function
\alpha\in{]0,1[}\mapsto\mu(\alpha)\in{]0,\infty[}
is strictly decreasing. Therefore, for two levels
0<\alpha<\alpha^{\prime}<1
, we have
\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\subset\mathcal{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
. Consider the function
\mathfrak{T}^{\bullet}_{\bm{S}}
that assigns to each given
\alpha\in{]0,1[}
the Wald test
\mathfrak{T}_{\bm{S}}^{\alpha}
. According to
27
and
Equation 159
, the p-value of
\mathfrak{T}^{\bullet}_{\bm{S}}
at some
\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{d}}
is given by:

\text{pval}_{\bm{S}}(\bm{x})=\inf\big\{\alpha\in{]0,1[}:\bm{x}\in\mathcal{S}_{\alpha}\big\}=\inf\big\{\alpha\in{]0,1[}:\mu(\alpha)<\nu_{\bm{S}}(\bm{x})\big\}\,.

(160)

Set
\alpha_{0}(\bm{x})=1-\mathbb{F}_{\chi^{2}_{\mathrm{d}}}(\nu_{\bm{S}}^{2}(\bm{x}))
.
Equation 35
implies that
\alpha_{0}(\bm{x})=1-\mathbb{F}_{\chi^{2}_{\mathrm{d}}}(\mu(\alpha_{0}(\bm{x}))^{2})
. Thereby, we have
\mu(\alpha_{0}(\bm{x}))=\nu_{\bm{S}}(\bm{x})
. Since
\mu
is decreasing and bijective, the foregoing implies that

\big\{\alpha\in{]0,1[}:\mu(\alpha)<\nu_{\bm{S}}(\bm{x})\big\}=\big\{\alpha\in{]0,1[}:\mu(\alpha)<\mu(\alpha_{0}(\bm{x}))\big\}={]\alpha_{0}(\bm{x}),1[}

We thus conclude from
Equation 160
that
\text{pval}_{\bm{S}}(\bm{x})=\alpha_{0}(\bm{x})=1-\mathbb{F}_{\chi^{2}_{\mathrm{d}}}(\nu_{\bm{S}}^{2}(\bm{x}))
.
